The moment envelope gives \(\lVert v_\theta\rVert_2\leq C_0\).  Since the support is nonempty and \(\text{ass:edge-gap}\) holds, some coordinate \(a=a(\theta)\) has \(|\zeta_a(\theta)|\geq\kappa\).  If \(j_a\) is the corresponding row of \(J_z(\theta)\), (9) implies \(\lVert j_a\rVert_2\geq\kappa/C_0\).  By \(\text{ass:variance-gap}\),
\[
 W_{aa}(\theta)=j_a\Omega(\theta)j_a^{\mathsf T}
 \geq\kappa\lVert j_a\rVert_2^2
 \geq c_\star:=\kappa^3/C_0^2>0.
\tag{10}
\]
The uniform derivative and long-run-variance bounds also give \(\lVert W(\theta)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_1\).

Let \(G\sim\mathcal N(0,W(\theta))\) and \(M=\max_{1\leq a\leq d}|G_a|\).  For \(0<\varepsilon<1\) and \(x\geq0\), if \(x\leq\sqrt\varepsilon\), (10) and the bounded density of its nondegenerate coordinate give
\[
 P(x<M\leq x+\varepsilon)
 \leq P\{|G_a|\leq\sqrt\varepsilon+\varepsilon\}
 \leq C_2\sqrt\varepsilon.
\]
If \(x>\sqrt\varepsilon\), the event requires at least one coordinate absolute value to lie in \((x,x+\varepsilon]\).  For a centered normal coordinate of variance \(s^2>0\), its absolute-value density at \(y>0\), maximized over \(s\), is at most \(C/y\); a zero-variance coordinate contributes zero.  A union bound gives
\[
 \sup_{\theta}\sup_{x\geq0}P_\theta(x<M\leq x+\varepsilon)
 \leq C_3d\sqrt\varepsilon.
\tag{11}
\]
Thus the Gaussian-maximum laws have a uniform continuous modulus despite possible singular covariance.

For a positive semidefinite matrix \(V\), set
\[
 F_V(x)=P\!\left\{\max_{a\leq d}|(V^{1/2}U)_a|\leq x\right\},
 \qquad U\sim\mathcal N(0,I_d),
\]
and use the generalized-inverse convention
\[
 c(V;1-\alpha)=\inf\{x\in[0,\infty):F_V(x)\geq1-\alpha\},
 \qquad \inf\varnothing=+\infty.
\tag{12}
\]
This defines the conditional critical value even for singular \(V\).  On the operator-norm bounded positive-semidefinite set, the square-root map is uniformly continuous: this follows by diagonalizing two matrices, approximating \(x\mapsto\sqrt x\) uniformly by polynomials on the common spectral interval, and using continuity of each polynomial.  Coupling \(\widehat W_n^{1/2}U\) and \(W(\theta)^{1/2}U\), then applying (7), (11), and Markov's inequality to \(\lVert U\rVert_2\), proves
\[
 \sup_{x\geq0}|F_{\widehat W_n}(x)-F_{W(\theta)}(x)|=o_{P_\theta}(1)
\tag{13}
\]
uniformly on \(\mathcal V_n\).

Let \(c_{n,1-\alpha}=c(\widehat W_n;1-\alpha)\), exactly as in the corrected \(\text{def:graph-confidence-set}\).  On the event that the left side of (13) is at most \(\delta\), the generalized-inverse definition implies
\[
 c_{n,1-\alpha}\geq
 \inf\{x:F_{W(\theta)}(x)\geq1-\alpha-\delta\}.
\tag{14}
\]
The vector bounded-Lipschitz approximation (4), with upper and lower Lipschitz approximations to the maximum rectangle and boundary error bounded by (11), applies at the deterministic threshold on the right of (14).  Letting first \(n\to\infty\) and then \(\delta\downarrow0\) yields
\[
 \inf_{\theta:P_\theta\in\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}(\kappa)}
 P_\theta\!\left[
 \max_{i<j}\left|\sqrt n\{\widehat z_{ij}-\zeta_{ij}(\theta)\}\right|
 \leq c_{n,1-\alpha},\ \mathcal V_n\right]
 \geq1-\alpha-o(1)-\sup_\theta P_\theta(\mathcal V_n^c).
\tag{15}
\]
On \(\mathcal V_n^c\), every interval is \(\mathbb R\), so simultaneous coordinate coverage is automatic.  Combining this fallback with (15) and uniform validity proves
\[
 \liminf_{n\to\infty}\inf_{\theta:P_\theta\in\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}(\kappa)}
 P_\theta\{\zeta_{ij}(\theta)\in I_{ij,n}\text{ for all }i<j\}
 \geq1-\alpha.
\tag{16}
\]

Finally, nonnegative excitation and acyclicity imply that at most one of \(A_{ij}\) and \(A_{ji}\) is positive.  Since every \(\lambda_i>0\), \(\zeta_{ij}>0\) means \(j\to i\), \(\zeta_{ij}<0\) means \(i\to j\), and \(\zeta_{ij}=0\) means neither edge.  On the event in (16), every true pairwise choice is admitted by the sign-inversion rules, and the true graph is acyclic; hence it belongs to \(\mathcal C_n(1-\alpha)\).  On \(\mathcal V_n^c\), that set contains every labeled acyclic graph.  Therefore
\[
 \liminf_{n\to\infty}\inf_{\theta:P_\theta\in\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}(\kappa)}
 P_\theta\{G(A)\in\mathcal C_n(1-\alpha)\}\geq1-\alpha.
\]
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The theorem calibrates the smooth signed jump before positive parts, proves direct Bartlett-sandwich consistency, and gives simultaneous graph inclusion with conservative total fallbacks. \textit{Gap.} A scaling-direction certificate supplies one uniformly nondegenerate Gaussian coordinate; a generalized-inverse maximum quantile and an explicit anti-concentration bound then cover singular covariance without studentization or exact-event-time scores. \textit{Consumer.} Alarm-network analysts can report compelled, excluded, and unresolved directions at one familywise level, with all invalid inverse samples mapped to the conservative all-graph set.

\begin{openendedquestion}[oeq:one-lag-minimality]\label{oeq:one-lag-minimality}
Using \(H_{\mathrm{one}}\), fix \(p=3\) and determine the injectivity locus of \(\mathcal O_1\) separately on every labeled chain, fork, and diamond support \(G_0\in\mathfrak G_3^{\mathrm{cfd}}\): for which \(\theta\in\Theta_{s,G_0}\) is \(\mathfrak F_1\{\mathcal O_1(\theta)\}\cap\Theta_{s,G_0}=\{\theta\}\)? For every \(s\in\{1,2,3\}\) and \(G_0\in\mathfrak G_3^{\mathrm{cfd}}\), determine whether there is a nonempty relatively open \(U=V\cap\Theta_{s,G_0}\), with \(V\) open under \(\rho_{\Theta}\), such that each \(\theta\in U\) admits \(\theta'\in\{\mathfrak F_1\{\mathcal O_1(\theta)\}\cap\Theta_{s,G_0}\}\setminus\{\theta\}\) with \(\rho_{\mathrm{law}}(P_{\theta},P_{\theta'})>0\).
\end{openendedquestion}
The rank strata and the complete fork fiber are settled by \(\text{prop:fork-one-lag-fiber}\): only \(s=1\) occurs for a fork, its one-lag fiber is a singleton exactly at the unique \(\beta_*\) satisfying \(e^{\beta_*\Delta}=1+2\beta_*\Delta\), and otherwise it has one observationally distinct alias on the opposite side of \(\beta_*\).  Only \(s=2\) can occur for a chain or transitive triangle.  Their exact injectivity loci and the existence or absence of relatively open alias regions remain unresolved.
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The fork stratum is now completely characterized, so the remaining information-order question concerns only the nonempty rank-two chain and transitive-triangle strata. \textit{Gap.} No cited full-information or full-spectrum theorem decides those legal fibers from only \(m\) and \(\Gamma_1\). \textit{Consumer.} The unresolved loci determine whether non-fork three-alarm archives must retain the second positive covariance lag.

\begin{openendedquestion}[oeq:honest-boundary-adaptation]\label{oeq:honest-boundary-adaptation}
Using \(H_{\mathrm{boundary}}\), fix \(p\), \(\Delta\), \(\alpha\), \(\kappa_0\), and a bandwidth sequence satisfying \(b_n\to\infty\) and \(b_n=o(n^{1/2})\). Construct a data-only \(\mathfrak U_n=(\mathcal G_n,\mathcal T_n)\in\mathfrak H_{n,\kappa_0}(\alpha)\) uniformly over the same nuisance-regular closure \(\overline{\mathcal M}_{\Delta,p}(\kappa_0)\), and characterize the sharp frontier \(\mathfrak R_{n,\kappa_0}(\delta_n)=(R_{G,n,\kappa_0}(\delta_n),R_{\Theta,n,\kappa_0}(\delta_n))\) for every sequence \(\delta_n\downarrow0\). Determine whether one honest procedure simultaneously attains both risks up to universal constants over every \(\mathcal M_{\kappa_0}(\delta_n)\), and quantify its excess graph-inclusion loss \(\ell_{\mathrm{inc}}\) relative to \(\mathcal C_n(1-\alpha)\) and its excess \(\rho_{\Theta}\)-diameter relative to \(\mathcal T_n^{\mathrm{fm}}(1-\alpha)\).
\end{openendedquestion}
If \(\theta_s=(\beta_s,0,\mu_0)\), \(s\in\{0,1\}\), are two admitted points of \(\overline{\mathcal M}_{\Delta,p}(\kappa_0)\) with \(\beta_0\neq\beta_1\), then their observable laws coincide and every finite-sample honest procedure obeys
\[
 P_0\!\left\{\operatorname{diam}_{\Theta}(\mathcal T_n)\geq1\wedge|\beta_1-\beta_0|\right\}\geq(1-2\alpha)_+,
 \qquad
 R_{\Theta,n,\kappa_0}(0)\geq(1-2\alpha)_+\{1\wedge|\beta_1-\beta_0|\}.
\]
Thus the empty-network boundary has a nonvanishing honest parameter-diameter obstruction whenever \(\alpha<1/2\). The sharp graph and parameter risks for positive \(\delta_n\downarrow0\) are not determined.
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The empty graph destroys decay identification and weak singular values destroy rank selection, so honest adaptation is the substantive unresolved inference boundary. \textit{Gap.} WangXieCuozzoMakXie2020 Section 2 and Theorem 3.2 constructs exact-event-time likelihood-score confidence polyhedra, while CheyssonLang2022 Theorems 2--3 impose conditions (A1)--(A4), including full-spectral identifiability (A2), for fixed-model Whittle limits. Neither covers rank, edge, or empty-network approach inside one nuisance-regular closure. GleserHwang1987 Section 2, Theorem 1, supplies the general warning that a nuisance limit erasing an unbounded target forces infinite confidence sets with positive probability, but whether and at what rate that mechanism governs this bounded-metric Hawkes frontier is unresolved. \textit{Consumer.} The result would tell cellular-network operators when a wide graph set is statistically unavoidable rather than evidence of a failed algorithm.

\begin{lemma}[lem:principal-log-strip]\label{lem:principal-log-strip}
If \(T=e^{\Delta B}\) and \(\operatorname{spec}(\Delta B)\subset\{z\in\mathbb C:|\operatorname{Im}z|<\pi\}\), then \(T\) has no eigenvalue on the closed negative real axis, its principal matrix logarithm exists, and \(\operatorname{Log}(T)=\Delta B\).
\end{lemma}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The classical principal-logarithm strip theorem is the only non-novel ingredient needed to remove matrix-exponential aliasing in the feedback branch. \textit{Gap.} This is classical matrix-function theory rather than a contribution of the note. \textit{Consumer.} The full-rank stable recovery theorem uses it to identify the continuous-time generator from the one-bin transition.

\begin{lemma}[lem:stable-covariance-semigroup]\label{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}
For every law in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\), \(B\) is a real Hurwitz matrix, \(Q\succ0\), \(C_{+}(t)=e^{Bt}K\) for \(t>0\), \(K=A(2\beta I_p-A)Q\), \(K-K^{\mathsf T}=AD-DA^{\mathsf T}\), and \(\Gamma_k=T^{k-1}J^2K\) for every \(k\geq1\). Moreover, \(J\) is invertible, \(\operatorname{im}(\Gamma_1)=\operatorname{im}(K)=\operatorname{im}(A)\), and \(\operatorname{rank}(\Gamma_1)=\operatorname{rank}(A)\).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write
\[
 \phi(u)=Ae^{-\beta u}\mathbf 1_{\{u>0\}},\qquad
 d\widetilde N(t)=dN(t)-\lambda\,dt,\qquad
 dM(t)=dN(t)-\ell(t)\,dt.
\tag{1}
\]
Fix a law in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\).  By \(\text{def:stable-common-exponential-class}\), \(\Delta>0\), \(\mu_i>0\), \(\beta>0\), \(A\) is entrywise nonnegative, and \(\rho(A/\beta)<1\).  Taking expectations in the nonnegative intensity equation is justified componentwise by monotone convergence.  Stationarity then gives
\[
 \lambda=\mu+\int_0^\infty Ae^{-\beta u}\lambda\,du
 =\mu+(A/\beta)\lambda.
\tag{2}
\]
In particular \(\lambda_i\geq\mu_i>0\) for every \(i\), so
\(D=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda)\succ0\).

If \(z\in\operatorname{spec}(A)\), then \(|z|\leq\rho(A)<\beta\).  Therefore every eigenvalue \(z-\beta\) of \(B=A-\beta I_p\) satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{Re}(z-\beta)\leq |z|-\beta<0.
\tag{3}
\]
Thus \(B\) is a real Hurwitz matrix.  In particular, there are constants \(c>0\) and \(C<\infty\) such that \(\|e^{Bu}\|\leq Ce^{-cu}\) for \(u\geq0\), after enlarging \(C\) to absorb the polynomial factors of the finitely many Jordan blocks.  Every improper matrix integral used below therefore converges absolutely.

Subtracting (2) from the intensity equation and using the definition of the compensated martingale measure in (1) gives the centered innovation equation
\[
 d\widetilde N=dM+\phi*d\widetilde N.
\tag{4}
\]
For every integer \(d\geq1\), induction and the beta integral give
\[
 \phi^{*d}(u)
 =A^d\frac{u^{d-1}}{(d-1)!}e^{-\beta u}\mathbf 1_{\{u>0\}}.
\tag{5}
\]
Moreover, \(\int_0^\infty\phi^{*d}(u)\,du=(A/\beta)^d\) entrywise.  Since \(A/\beta\geq0\) and its spectral radius is less than one, the matrix geometric series converges.  Hence the entrywise \(L^1\) resolvent exists and (5) sums to
\[
 \psi(u):=\sum_{d=1}^{\infty}\phi^{*d}(u)
 =e^{-\beta u}e^{Au}A=e^{Bu}A,\quad u>0.
\tag{6}
\]
Iteration of (4) therefore yields, on every bounded interval in second mean,
\[
 d\widetilde N=dM+\psi*dM.
\tag{7}
\]
Indeed, the predictable quadratic covariation of \(M\) is \(D\,dt\), so the compensated-integral isometry reduces convergence of the remainder to the entrywise \(L^1\) convergence above together with the finite intensity in (2).

We next compute the positive-lag covariance with the orientation in the statement: the future coordinate is the row and the time-zero coordinate is the column.  For \(t>0\), causality in (7), orthogonality of martingale increments on disjoint intervals, and the predictable covariance measure \(D\,dt\) give
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 C_{+}(t)
 &=\psi(t)D+
   \int_{-\infty}^{0}\psi(t-s)D\psi(-s)^{\mathsf T}\,ds\\
 &=\psi(t)D+\int_0^\infty\psi(t+u)D\psi(u)^{\mathsf T}\,du.
 \end{aligned}
\tag{8}
\]
Simplicity supplies the separate diagonal atom \(D\delta_0\); it does not enter (8), which is the nonsingular density at \(t>0\).  Substituting (6), and using that \(A\), \(B\), and \(e^{Bu}\) commute, yields
\[
 C_{+}(t)
 =e^{Bt}\left\{AD+AQA^{\mathsf T}\right\},\quad
 Q:=\int_0^\infty e^{Bu}De^{B^{\mathsf T}u}\,du.
\tag{9}
\]
For every nonzero \(x\in\mathbb R^p\),
\[
 x^{\mathsf T}Qx
 =\int_0^\infty
 \left\|D^{1/2}e^{B^{\mathsf T}u}x\right\|_2^2,du>0,
\tag{10}
\]
because the continuous integrand at \(u=0\) equals \(\|D^{1/2}x\|_2^2>0\).  Thus \(Q\succ0\).  Differentiating the integrand defining \(Q\), integrating over \([0,\infty)\), and using the Hurwitz limit gives
\[
 BQ+QB^{\mathsf T}=-D,\quad
 D=2\beta Q-AQ-QA^{\mathsf T}.
\tag{11}
\]
Consequently the bracket in (9) is
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 K&:=AD+AQA^{\mathsf T}\\
 &=A(2\beta Q-AQ-QA^{\mathsf T})+AQA^{\mathsf T}\\
 &=A(2\beta I_p-A)Q,
 \end{aligned}
\tag{12}
\]
and (9) proves \(C_{+}(t)=e^{Bt}K\).  Since \(AQA^{\mathsf T}\) is symmetric, the first expression in (12) also gives
\[
 K-K^{\mathsf T}=AD-DA^{\mathsf T}.
\tag{13}
\]

Fix \(k\geq1\).  The bins \([0,\Delta)\) and \([k\Delta,(k+1)\Delta)\) are disjoint; for \(k=1\) their common endpoint has zero covariance measure.  Parametrize the later time by \(k\Delta+u\) and the earlier time by \(v\), with \(u,v\in[0,\Delta]\).  By \(\text{def:population-moments}\), stationarity, (9), and Fubini's theorem,
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \Gamma_k
 &=\int_0^\Delta\!\int_0^\Delta
 e^{B(k\Delta+u-v)}K\,du\,dv\\
 &=e^{B(k-1)\Delta}
 \left(\int_0^\Delta e^{Bu}\,du\right)
 \left(\int_0^\Delta e^{B(\Delta-v)}\,dv\right)K\\
 &=T^{k-1}J^2K.
 \end{aligned}
\tag{14}
\]
All uses of Fubini are valid because the integrands are continuous on the compact bin square.

It remains to prove the image assertions.  Since \(B\) is Hurwitz, it is invertible.  Spectral mapping and (3) show that every eigenvalue of \(T=e^{B\Delta}\) has modulus strictly less than one; hence \(T-I_p\) is invertible.  Therefore
\[
 J=\int_0^\Delta e^{Bu}\,du=B^{-1}(T-I_p)
\tag{15}
\]
is invertible.  Also \(2\beta I_p-A\) is invertible: if \(z\in\operatorname{spec}(A)\), then \(|z|<\beta<2\beta\).  Since \(Q\) is invertible by (10), (12) implies
\[
 \operatorname{im}(K)=\operatorname{im}(A).
\tag{16}
\]
The matrices \(J\) and \(A\) commute because \(J\) is an integral of functions of \(B=A-\beta I_p\); their inverses commute as well.  Hence \(J^2\) maps \(\operatorname{im}(A)\) bijectively onto itself.  Equations (14) at \(k=1\) and (16) now yield
\[
 \operatorname{im}(\Gamma_1)=J^2\operatorname{im}(K)
 =J^2\operatorname{im}(A)=\operatorname{im}(A).
\tag{17}
\]
Together, (16)--(17) prove \(\operatorname{im}(\Gamma_1)=\operatorname{im}(K)=\operatorname{im}(A)\), and taking dimensions proves \(\operatorname{rank}(\Gamma_1)=\operatorname{rank}(A)\).
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The semigroup and image identity are the common algebraic substrate for every closed-form branch and for reconstructing the complete response in the broad Wiener--Hopf inverse. \textit{Gap.} Published complete-response and full-spectrum results do not state this exact fixed-bin semigroup, jump factorization, or image equality for a stable common-exponential matrix. \textit{Consumer.} The broad nonzero stable recovery theorem and all four explicit algebraic branches use this lemma to recover the active transition and undo bin integration.

\begin{proposition}[prop:empty-graph-honest-diameter-obstruction]\label{prop:empty-graph-honest-diameter-obstruction}
Fix \(n\geq3\). Let \(\theta_s=(\beta_s,0,\mu_0)\), \(s\in\{0,1\}\), have a common strictly positive baseline \(\mu_0\), distinct decays \(\beta_0\neq\beta_1\), and satisfy \(P_{\theta_s}\in\overline{\mathcal M}_{\Delta,p}(\kappa_0)\). Put \(d_{01}=1\wedge|\beta_1-\beta_0|\). Then \(P_{\theta_0}=P_{\theta_1}=:P_0\), and every \(\mathfrak U_n=(\mathcal G_n,\mathcal T_n)\in\mathfrak H_{n,\kappa_0}(\alpha)\) satisfies
\[
 P_0\!\left\{\operatorname{diam}_{\Theta}(\mathcal T_n)\geq d_{01}\right\}\geq(1-2\alpha)_+.
\]
Consequently,
\[
 R_{\Theta,n,\kappa_0}(0)\geq d_{01}(1-2\alpha)_+.
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For each \(s\in\{0,1\}\), the excitation matrix in \(\theta_s\) is zero. By \(\text{prop:empty-graph-equivalence}\), under \(P_{\theta_s}\) the coordinates \(N_1,\ldots,N_p\) are independent homogeneous Poisson processes with respective rates \(\mu_{0,1},\ldots,\mu_{0,p}\), and their observable law is independent of \(\beta_s\). The common baseline therefore gives
\[
 P_{\theta_0}=P_{\theta_1}=:P_0.
\]

Fix \(n\geq3\) and \(\mathfrak U_n=(\mathcal G_n,\mathcal T_n)\in\mathfrak H_{n,\kappa_0}(\alpha)\). For \(s\in\{0,1\}\), define
\[
 B_s=\{G(0)\in\mathcal G_n,\ \theta_s\in\mathcal T_n\}.
\]
The definition of \(\mathfrak H_{n,\kappa_0}(\alpha)\), applied separately to the two admitted parameter points \(\theta_s\), yields
\[
 P_0(B_s)=P_{\theta_s}(B_s)\geq1-\alpha,
 \qquad s\in\{0,1\}.
\]
The elementary union bound and nonnegativity of probability give
\[
 P_0(B_0\cap B_1)
 \geq P_0(B_0)+P_0(B_1)-1
 \geq1-2\alpha,
\]
and hence \(P_0(B_0\cap B_1)\geq(1-2\alpha)_+\). On \(B_0\cap B_1\), both \(\theta_0\) and \(\theta_1\) belong to \(\mathcal T_n\). By the definitions of \(\rho_{\Theta}\) and \(\operatorname{diam}_{\Theta}\),
\[
 \operatorname{diam}_{\Theta}(\mathcal T_n)
 \geq \rho_{\Theta}(\theta_0,\theta_1)
 =1\wedge\left\{|\beta_1-\beta_0|+\lVert0-0\rVert_{\mathrm F}+\lVert\mu_0-\mu_0\rVert_2\right\}
 =d_{01}.
\]
This proves the probability assertion. Since the diameter is nonnegative,
\[
 \mathbb E_0\!\left[\operatorname{diam}_{\Theta}(\mathcal T_n)\right]
 \geq d_{01}P_0\!\left\{\operatorname{diam}_{\Theta}(\mathcal T_n)\geq d_{01}\right\}
 \geq d_{01}(1-2\alpha)_+.
\]
Finally, \(A=0\) implies \(\delta(\theta_s)=0\) by \(\text{def:boundary-risks}\), while the assumed memberships place the common law in \(\mathcal M_{\kappa_0}(0)\). Thus, for every fixed honest \(\mathfrak U_n\), the supremum defining \(R_{\Theta,n,\kappa_0}(0)\) is at least the last display. Taking the infimum over \(\mathfrak U_n\in\mathfrak H_{n,\kappa_0}(\alpha)\) preserves this lower bound and proves the conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[prop:fork-one-lag-fiber]\label{prop:fork-one-lag-fiber}
For \(p=3\), every labeled fork support has \(\operatorname{rank}(A)=\operatorname{rank}(\Gamma_1)=1\), whereas every labeled directed-chain support and every labeled three-node diamond support, meaning the transitive triangle in \(\mathfrak G_3^{\mathrm{cfd}}\), has rank two; consequently all other rank-support strata are empty. Fix a fork and relabel it as \(1\to2\) and \(1\to3\), with \(A_{21}=a>0\) and \(A_{31}=b>0\). Put \(x=a/\beta\), \(y=b/\beta\), and \(h(\beta)=(1-e^{-\beta\Delta})^2/\beta\). On this fork stratum, \((m,\Gamma_1)\) identifies \(x\), \(y\), and \(h(\beta)\). The function \(h\) has a unique maximizer \(\beta_\star=z_\star/\Delta\), where \(z_\star>0\) is the unique positive solution of \(e^{z_\star}=1+2z_\star\). The within-support one-lag fiber is a singleton exactly when \(\beta=\beta_\star\), and otherwise consists of exactly two parameter points, one on each side of \(\beta_\star\). The two points induce different laws because their second positive covariance lags differ. Thus each of \(\{\theta\in\Theta_{1,G_0}:\beta<\beta_\star\}\) and \(\{\theta\in\Theta_{1,G_0}:\beta>\beta_\star\}\) is a nonempty relatively open one-lag alias region for every labeled fork \(G_0\).
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Rank is unchanged by relabeling, so choose canonical labels.  A directed chain has positive amplitudes in positions \((2,1)\) and \((3,2)\); its first two columns are linearly independent, and hence its excitation matrix has rank two.  A transitive triangle has positive entries in positions \((2,1),(3,1),(3,2)\); again its first two columns are linearly independent, so its rank is two.  A fork may be written
\[
 A_{21}=a>0,\qquad A_{31}=b>0,
\]
with all other entries zero, and its two nonzero rows are proportional; hence its rank is one.  By \(\text{lem:covariance-semigroup}\), \(\operatorname{rank}(\Gamma_1)=\operatorname{rank}(A)\).  This proves every rank-stratum assertion.

For the fork, \(A^2=0\).  Put
\[
 x=a/\beta,\qquad y=b/\beta,\qquad
 c(\beta)=\frac{1-e^{-\beta\Delta}}{\beta}.
\tag{1}
\]
The Lyapunov integral from \(\text{lem:covariance-semigroup}\), using \(e^{Bu}=e^{-\beta u}(I_p+Au)\), gives
\[
 Q=\frac{D}{2\beta}+\frac{AD+DA^{\mathsf T}}{4\beta^2}
   +\frac{ADA^{\mathsf T}}{4\beta^3}.
\tag{2}
\]
Substitution into \(K=A(2\beta I_p-A)Q\), or equivalently into \(K=AD+AQA^{\mathsf T}\), yields
\[
 K_{21}=a\lambda_1,\quad K_{31}=b\lambda_1,\qquad
 K_{22}=\frac{a^2\lambda_1}{2\beta},\qquad
 K_{23}=K_{32}=\frac{ab\lambda_1}{2\beta},\qquad
 K_{33}=\frac{b^2\lambda_1}{2\beta},
\tag{3}
\]
and all other entries are zero.  In particular \(AK=0\).  Since
\[
 J=\int_0^\Delta e^{-\beta u}(I_p+Au)\,du=c(\beta)I_p+d(\beta)A
\]
for a scalar \(d(\beta)\), equations \(A^2=0\) and \(AK=0\) imply
\[
 \Gamma_1=J^2K=c(\beta)^2K.
\tag{4}
\]

The one-lag observable contains \(m\), so it identifies \(\lambda=m/\Delta\); every \(\lambda_i\) is positive.  From (3)--(4),
\[
 x=\frac{2(\Gamma_1)_{22}}{(\Gamma_1)_{21}},\qquad
 y=\frac{2(\Gamma_1)_{33}}{(\Gamma_1)_{31}},\qquad
 h(\beta):=\frac{(1-e^{-\beta\Delta})^2}{\beta}
 =\frac{(\Gamma_1)_{21}}{x\lambda_1}.
\tag{5}
\]
All divisions in (5) are valid because \(a,b,\lambda_1,c(\beta)>0\).  Conversely, \((\lambda,x,y,h(\beta))\) reconstructs every entry in (3)--(4).  It also fixes
\[
 \mu=(I_p-A/\beta)\lambda
 =(\lambda_1,\lambda_2-x\lambda_1,\lambda_3-y\lambda_1)^{\mathsf T}.
\tag{6}
\]
Thus a candidate decay \(\widetilde\beta>0\) gives a legal point in the same one-lag fiber exactly when \(h(\widetilde\beta)=h(\beta)\), with amplitudes \(\widetilde a=x\widetilde\beta\), \(\widetilde b=y\widetilde\beta\), and the same \(\lambda,\mu\).  Positivity in (6) is preserved because its right side does not depend on the candidate decay.

Set \(z=\beta\Delta\).  Since
\[
 h(\beta)=\Delta\frac{(1-e^{-z})^2}{z},
\]
logarithmic differentiation gives
\[
 \frac{d}{dz}\log\frac{(1-e^{-z})^2}{z}
 =\frac{2}{e^z-1}-\frac1z.
\tag{7}
\]
The derivative in (7) vanishes exactly when \(e^z=1+2z\).  The function \(e^z-1-2z\) is strictly convex, is zero at \(z=0\), has derivative \(-1\) there, and tends to infinity.  It therefore has exactly one further zero \(z_*>0\).  Equation (7) is positive on \((0,z_*)\) and negative on \((z_*,\infty)\).  Moreover \(h(\beta)\to0\) at both endpoints \(0\) and \(\infty\).  Hence every value strictly below \(h(\beta_*)\) has exactly one preimage on each side of \(\beta_*=z_*/\Delta\), whereas the maximum has the single preimage \(\beta_*\).  Together with (5)--(6), this proves the asserted fiber cardinalities and injectivity locus.

For any alias \(\theta'\neq\theta\), (4) and \(A\Gamma_1=0\) give
\[
 \Gamma_2=T\Gamma_1=e^{-\beta\Delta}(I_p+A\Delta)\Gamma_1
 =e^{-\beta\Delta}\Gamma_1.
\tag{8}
\]
Because \(\Gamma_1\neq0\) and \(\beta'\neq\beta\), the two laws have different second-lag covariances by (8).  Their finite-block binned distributions therefore cannot all agree, so the definition of \(\rho_{\mathrm{law}}\) gives \(\rho_{\mathrm{law}}(P_\theta,P_{\theta'})>0\).

Finally, \(\beta\mapsto\beta\) is continuous in \(\rho_\Theta\), so the two strict-inequality subsets are relatively open.  They are nonempty: for any \(x,y>0\) and any decay on the desired side of \(\beta_*\), choose \(\lambda_1>0\), \(\lambda_2>x\lambda_1\), and \(\lambda_3>y\lambda_1\), then define \(a=x\beta\), \(b=y\beta\), and \(\mu\) by (6).  This produces a positive-baseline stationary acyclic Hawkes parameter in the required subset.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The proposition is the exact computable side of the one-lag question and gives an explicit generic two-point alias witness. \textit{Gap.} Existing Hawkes identification papers do not classify this fixed-support one-lag fiber. \textit{Consumer.} A fork archive needs the second lag except at the unique scalar maximizer.

\begin{theorem}[thm:full-rank-stable-two-lag-recovery]\label{thm:full-rank-stable-two-lag-recovery}
For every \(\eta=(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\in\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{fr}}\), all quantities in \(\text{def:full-rank-two-lag-inverse}\) are well defined and independent of the structural representative in \(\text{def:full-rank-stable-moment-image}\). In particular, \(\Gamma_1\), \(B^\dagger\), \(J^\dagger\), \(K^\dagger\), and \(Q^\dagger\) are invertible, \(B^\dagger\) is real Hurwitz, \(Q^\dagger\succ0\) is the unique solution of \(B^\dagger Q+Q(B^\dagger)^{\mathsf T}=-D^\dagger\), and \(s^\dagger>0\). For every structural representative \((\beta,A,\mu)\),
\[
 T^\dagger=T,\quad B^\dagger=B,\quad J^\dagger=J,\quad K^\dagger=K,\quad Q^\dagger=Q,\quad s^\dagger=\beta^2,
\]
and \(\Phi^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\Delta,p}(\eta)=(\beta,A,\mu)\). Thus the mean and two positive covariance lags identify stable full-rank cyclic, reciprocal, and self-exciting common-exponential networks on the stated principal-log no-alias strip.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(\eta=(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\in\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{fr}}\).  By \(\text{def:full-rank-stable-moment-image}\), choose an arbitrary admissible structural representative \((\beta,A,\mu)\), put \(B=A-\beta I_p\), and note that
\[
 \operatorname{spec}(\Delta B)\subset
 \{z\in\mathbb C:|\operatorname{Im}z|<\pi\}.
\tag{1}
\]
By \(\text{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}\), \(B\) is real Hurwitz, \(Q\succ0\), \(J\) is invertible, and
\[
 \Gamma_1=J^2K,\qquad \Gamma_2=TJ^2K,\qquad
 T=e^{B\Delta},\qquad K=A(2\beta I_p-A)Q.
\tag{2}
\]
Membership in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\) gives \(\rho(A/\beta)<1\) and \(\beta>0\).  Hence every \(z\in\operatorname{spec}(A)\) satisfies \(|z|<\beta\), so \(2\beta-z\neq0\).  Thus \(2\beta I_p-A\) is invertible.  The definition of \(\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{fr}}\) gives \(\det(A)\neq0\), while \(Q\succ0\) gives invertibility of \(Q\).  The product in (2) therefore makes \(K\) invertible.  Since \(J\) is invertible, \(\Gamma_1=J^2K\) is invertible.  Consequently
\[
 T^\dagger=\Gamma_2\Gamma_1^{-1}
 =TJ^2K(J^2K)^{-1}=T=e^{B\Delta}.
\tag{3}
\]
By (1), (3), and \(\text{lem:principal-log-strip}\), the principal logarithm exists and
\[
 B^\dagger=\Delta^{-1}\operatorname{Log}(T^\dagger)
 =\Delta^{-1}\operatorname{Log}(e^{B\Delta})=B.
\tag{4}
\]
Division by \(\Delta\) is valid because \(\Delta>0\).  Thus \(B^\dagger\) is real Hurwitz and in particular invertible.  Its exponential has all eigenvalues strictly inside the unit disk, so \(T^\dagger-I_p\) is invertible.  The definition of \(J^\dagger\), (3), and (4) give
\[
 J^\dagger=(B^\dagger)^{-1}(T^\dagger-I_p)
 =B^{-1}(T-I_p)=J,
 \qquad
 K^\dagger=(J^\dagger)^{-2}\Gamma_1=J^{-2}J^2K=K.
\tag{5}
\]
In particular, \(J^\dagger\) and \(K^\dagger\) are invertible.

Stationarity and \(\text{def:population-moments}\) yield
\[
 m=\mathbb E\{N(\Delta)-N(0)\}=\Delta\lambda.
\tag{6}
\]
Hence \(\lambda^\dagger=m/\Delta=\lambda\) and \(D^\dagger=D\succ0\).  Because \(B^\dagger=B\) is Hurwitz, there are \(c>0\) and \(M<\infty\) such that \(\|e^{Bu}\|\leq Me^{-cu}\) for \(u\geq0\).  Therefore the integral defining \(Q^\dagger\) converges absolutely, and
\[
 Q^\dagger=\int_0^\infty e^{Bu}De^{B^{\mathsf T}u}\,du=Q\succ0.
\tag{7}
\]
Differentiating its integrand and using the Hurwitz limit gives
\[
 BQ^\dagger+Q^\dagger B^{\mathsf T}
 =\int_0^\infty\frac{d}{du}
   \{e^{Bu}De^{B^{\mathsf T}u}\}\,du=-D.
\tag{8}
\]
This Lyapunov solution is unique: if \(H_0\) is the difference of two solutions, then \(BH_0+H_0B^{\mathsf T}=0\), so \(e^{Bu}H_0e^{B^{\mathsf T}u}=H_0\) for every \(u\geq0\); the Hurwitz limit of the left side is zero, and hence \(H_0=0\).  Equations (4), (6), and (8) give the asserted Lyapunov characterization with \(B^\dagger\) and \(D^\dagger\).

Since \(A=B+\beta I_p\), the identity for \(K\) in (2) becomes
\[
 K=(B+\beta I_p)(\beta I_p-B)Q=(\beta^2I_p-B^2)Q.
\tag{9}
\]
Using (4), (5), (7), and (9),
\[
 K^\dagger(Q^\dagger)^{-1}+(B^\dagger)^2=\beta^2I_p.
\tag{10}
\]
Taking traces and using \(p\geq2\) gives
\[
 s^\dagger=p^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\beta^2I_p)=\beta^2>0.
\tag{11}
\]
The inverse selects the positive real square root and \(\beta>0\); consequently \(\beta^\dagger=\beta\), and then
\[
 A^\dagger=B^\dagger+\beta^\dagger I_p=B+\beta I_p=A.
\tag{12}
\]

Finally, taking expectations in the common-exponential Hawkes intensity is justified componentwise by monotone convergence, because the kernel and point measures are nonnegative.  Stationarity, \(\beta>0\), and finiteness of \(\lambda\) give
\[
 \lambda=\mu+\int_0^\infty Ae^{-\beta u}\lambda\,du
 =\mu+(A/\beta)\lambda.
\tag{13}
\]
Thus, by (6), (11), and (12),
\[
 \mu^\dagger=(I_p-A^\dagger/\beta^\dagger)\lambda^\dagger
 =(I_p-A/\beta)\lambda=\mu.
\tag{14}
\]
The representative was arbitrary, whereas every daggered quantity is a function only of \(\eta\) and the known \(\Delta\).  Equations (3)--(14) therefore hold for every admissible representative and force all such representatives to coincide.  They prove every claimed inverse, positivity, well-definedness, representative-independence, and exact-recovery assertion.  Since \(\text{def:stable-common-exponential-class}\) imposes no acyclicity, zero-diagonal, orientation, reciprocity, or self-loop restriction, the conclusion covers the stated full-rank cyclic, reciprocal, and self-exciting members of the image domain.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The theorem extends exact finite-two-lag recovery to feedback networks under full rank and a transparent no-alias condition without weakening the singular DAG result. \textit{Gap.} Broad cyclic Hawkes identification uses richer full-lag or spectral information, while the existing acyclic theorem excludes feedback. \textit{Consumer.} Stable full-rank alarm networks with reciprocal or self-exciting channels obtain an explicit two-lag inverse.

\begin{theorem}[thm:restricted-rank-stable-two-lag-recovery]\label{thm:restricted-rank-stable-two-lag-recovery}
For every \(\eta=(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\in\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{0,+,\mathrm{rs}}\), every quantity in \(\text{def:restricted-rank-two-lag-inverse}\) is well defined, independent of the orthonormal basis \(O\), and independent of the admissible structural representative.  More precisely, \(r\geq1\), \(R\) has full row rank, the principal logarithm of \(F\) exists, \(D^\sharp\succ0\), \(W^\sharp\succ0\), and \(L^\sharp\) and \(F-I_r\) are invertible.  For every admissible representative \((\beta,A,\mu)\), \(F\) is the coordinate matrix of \(T|_S\), \(L^\sharp\) is the coordinate matrix of \(B|_S\), \(\beta^\sharp=\beta\), \(A_S^\sharp=O^{\mathsf T}AO\), \(K^\sharp=K\), \(\mathsf C^\sharp=AD\), and
\[
 \Phi_{\Delta,p}^{0,+,\mathrm{rs}}(\eta)=(\beta,A,\mu).
\]
Consequently the mean and two positive covariance lags identify every amplitude, baseline, decay, and labeled edge for every nonempty stable zero-diagonal common-exponential model satisfying the principal-log strip only on \(S=\operatorname{im}(A)=\operatorname{im}(\Gamma_1)\), including singular directed cycles and reciprocal pairs.  These cases are genuine: for every \(\Delta>0\), taking \(\beta=1\), \(\mu=(1,1,1,1)^{\mathsf T}\), \(\varepsilon_\Delta=\min\{1/2,\pi/(2\sqrt3\Delta)\}\), and
\[
 A=\begin{pmatrix}
 0&0&\varepsilon_\Delta&0\\
 \varepsilon_\Delta&0&0&0\\
 0&\varepsilon_\Delta&0&0\\
 0&0&0&0
 \end{pmatrix}
\]
gives a stable rank-three directed cycle with an isolated label in dimension four, while taking \(\beta=1\), \(\mu=(1,1,1)^{\mathsf T}\), and
\[
 A=\begin{pmatrix}0&1/2&0\\1/2&0&0\\0&0&0\end{pmatrix}
\]
gives a stable rank-two reciprocal pair with an isolated label in dimension three; both satisfy the stated restricted strip and belong to the image domain.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(\eta\in\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{0,+,\mathrm{rs}}\) and choose an arbitrary admissible representative \((\beta,A,\mu)\).  Put \(B=A-\beta I_p\), \(T=e^{B\Delta}\), and use the remaining notation of \(\text{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}\).  That lemma gives
\[
 B\text{ is Hurwitz},\quad D\succ0,\quad J\text{ is invertible},
 \quad \Gamma_1=J^2K,\quad \Gamma_2=T\Gamma_1,
\tag{1}
\]
\[
 S=\operatorname{im}(\Gamma_1)=\operatorname{im}(K)=\operatorname{im}(A),
 \quad r=\operatorname{rank}(\Gamma_1)=\operatorname{rank}(A).
\tag{2}
\]
The image-domain condition \(A\neq0\) and (2) imply \(r\geq1\).  Since \(A\operatorname{im}(A)\subseteq\operatorname{im}(A)\), the space \(S\) is invariant under \(A\), hence under \(B\), \(T\), and \(J\).

Choose the basis \(O\) prescribed by the inverse and put \(R=O^{\mathsf T}\Gamma_1\).  Equation (2) implies \(\Gamma_1=OR\).  Because \(\Gamma_1\) has rank \(r\) and \(O\) has \(r\) orthonormal columns, \(R\) has row rank \(r\); therefore \(RR^{\mathsf T}\succ0\) and its inverse exists.  Invariance gives a unique \(F_0\in\mathbb R^{r\times r}\) such that \(TO=OF_0\).  From (1),
\[
 \Gamma_2=T\Gamma_1=TO R=OF_0R.
\]
Multiplication by \(O^{\mathsf T}\) on the left and by the right inverse \(R^{\mathsf T}(RR^{\mathsf T})^{-1}\) on the right proves
\[
 F=O^{\mathsf T}\Gamma_2R^{\mathsf T}(RR^{\mathsf T})^{-1}=F_0.
\tag{3}
\]
Thus \(F\) represents \(T|_S\).  Define
\[
 L_0=O^{\mathsf T}BO,\quad A_{S,0}=O^{\mathsf T}AO.
\]
Then \(BO=OL_0\), \(AO=OA_{S,0}\), and
\[
 L_0=A_{S,0}-\beta I_r,\quad F=e^{\Delta L_0}.
\tag{4}
\]
The restricted-strip condition in \(\text{def:restricted-rank-stable-moment-image}\), (3)--(4), and \(\text{lem:principal-log-strip}\), applied in dimension \(r\), imply that the principal logarithm exists and
\[
 L^\sharp=\Delta^{-1}\operatorname{Log}(F)=L_0.
\tag{5}
\]
The restriction of a Hurwitz matrix to an invariant subspace is Hurwitz, so \(L^\sharp=L_0\) is invertible.  By spectral mapping, every eigenvalue of \(F=e^{\Delta L_0}\) has modulus strictly less than one; hence \(F-I_r\) is invertible.

Complete \(O\) to an orthogonal matrix \(\mathcal U=(O,O_\perp)\).  Because \(\operatorname{im}(A)=S\),
\[
 \mathcal U^{\mathsf T}A\mathcal U
 =\begin{pmatrix}A_{S,0}&*\\0&0\end{pmatrix}.
\tag{6}
\]
The image definition imposes \(\operatorname{diag}(A)=0\), so \(\operatorname{tr}(A)=0\).  Taking traces in (6), and then in (4), yields
\[
 \operatorname{tr}(A_{S,0})=\operatorname{tr}(A)=0,
 \quad \operatorname{tr}(L_0)=-r\beta.
\tag{7}
\]
Since \(r>0\), (5)--(7) prove
\[
 \beta^\sharp=-\operatorname{tr}(L^\sharp)/r=\beta>0,
 \quad A_S^\sharp=L^\sharp+\beta^\sharp I_r=A_{S,0}.
\tag{8}
\]

Let \(J_S=O^{\mathsf T}JO\), the coordinate matrix of \(J|_S\).  Integrating \(e^{Bu}O=Oe^{L_0u}\), and using the invertibility of \(L_0\), gives
\[
 J_S=\int_0^\Delta e^{L_0u}\,du=L_0^{-1}(F-I_r).
\tag{9}
\]
Equations (5) and (9) imply
\[
 H^\sharp=L^\sharp(F-I_r)^{-1}=J_S^{-1}.
\tag{10}
\]
The columns of \(K\) lie in \(S\) by (2), so write \(K=OK_S\), where \(K_S=O^{\mathsf T}K\).  From (1), invariance, and \(\Gamma_1=OR\),
\[
 R=O^{\mathsf T}\Gamma_1=O^{\mathsf T}J^2OK_S=J_S^2K_S.
\tag{11}
\]
Consequently, by (10)--(11),
\[
 K^\sharp=O(H^\sharp)^2R=OJ_S^{-2}J_S^2K_S=OK_S=K.
\tag{12}
\]
The antisymmetric-jump identity in \(\text{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}\) now gives
\[
 Z^\sharp=K-K^{\mathsf T}=AD-DA^{\mathsf T}.
\tag{13}
\]

The mean identity follows directly from stationarity:
\[
 m=\mathbb E\{N(\Delta)-N(0)\}=\Delta\lambda.
\tag{14}
\]
Thus \(\lambda^\sharp=\lambda\) and \(D^\sharp=D\succ0\), so \(D^\sharp\) is invertible.  Also, for every nonzero \(x\in\mathbb R^r\),
\[
 x^{\mathsf T}W^\sharp x
 =(Ox)^{\mathsf T}D^{-1}(Ox)>0,
\tag{15}
\]
because \(D^{-1}\succ0\) and \(O\) has full column rank.  Hence \(W^\sharp\succ0\) and is invertible.

It remains to verify the nontrivial block reconstruction.  Put \(P=OO^{\mathsf T}\) and \(C_0=AD\).  Since \(D\) is invertible, \(\operatorname{im}(C_0)=\operatorname{im}(A)=S\); hence
\[
 PC_0=C_0,\quad (I_p-P)C_0=0.
\tag{16}
\]
By (13), \(Z^\sharp=C_0-C_0^{\mathsf T}\).  Multiplying this identity on the right by \(I_p-P\), and using \(C_0^{\mathsf T}(I_p-P)=\{(I_p-P)C_0\}^{\mathsf T}=0\), gives
\[
 C_0(I_p-P)=Z^\sharp(I_p-P).
\tag{17}
\]
Furthermore, (8) gives \(AO=OA_S^\sharp\), so
\[
 C_0D^{-1}O=AO=OA_S^\sharp.
\tag{18}
\]
Since
\[
 D^{-1}O=PD^{-1}O+(I_p-P)D^{-1}O
 =O\{O^{\mathsf T}D^{-1}O\}+(I_p-P)D^{-1}O
 =OW^\sharp+(I_p-P)D^{-1}O,
\]
substitution into (18), followed by (17), yields
\[
 C_0O W^\sharp
 =OA_S^\sharp-Z^\sharp(I_p-P)D^{-1}O.
\tag{19}
\]
Right multiplication by \((W^\sharp)^{-1}\) is valid by (15).  Combining (16)--(19),
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 C_0
 &=C_0P+C_0(I_p-P)\\
 &=\{OA_S^\sharp-Z^\sharp(I_p-P)D^{-1}O\}
   (W^\sharp)^{-1}O^{\mathsf T}+Z^\sharp(I_p-P)
 =\mathsf C^\sharp.
 \end{aligned}
\tag{20}
\]
Therefore
\[
 A^\sharp=\mathsf C^\sharp D^{-1}=AD D^{-1}=A.
\tag{21}
\]
Taking expectations in the nonnegative Hawkes intensity and using monotone convergence, stationarity, and \(\beta>0\),
\[
 \lambda=\mu+\int_0^\infty Ae^{-\beta u}\lambda\,du
 =\mu+(A/\beta)\lambda.
\tag{22}
\]
Equations (8), (14), (21), and (22) imply
\[
 \mu^\sharp=(I_p-A^\sharp/\beta^\sharp)\lambda^\sharp=\mu.
\tag{23}
\]

We next prove basis invariance.  Let \(O'=OU\) for an orthogonal \(U\in\mathbb R^{r\times r}\).  Direct substitution gives
\[
 R'=U^{\mathsf T}R,\quad F'=U^{\mathsf T}FU.
\tag{24}
\]
The principal logarithm is preserved by similarity, by uniqueness of the principal logarithm, so
\[
 L^{\sharp\prime}=U^{\mathsf T}L^\sharp U,\quad
 \beta^{\sharp\prime}=\beta^\sharp,\quad
 A_S^{\sharp\prime}=U^{\mathsf T}A_S^\sharp U,\quad
 H^{\sharp\prime}=U^{\mathsf T}H^\sharp U.
\tag{25}
\]
Equations (24)--(25) show \(O'(H^{\sharp\prime})^2R'=O(H^\sharp)^2R\), so \(K^\sharp\) and \(Z^\sharp\) are invariant.  Also
\[
 P^{\sharp\prime}=P^\sharp,\quad
 W^{\sharp\prime}=U^{\mathsf T}W^\sharp U.
\tag{26}
\]
Writing
\[
 V_0=OA_S^\sharp-Z^\sharp(I_p-P^\sharp)D^{-1}O,
\]
the corresponding primed bracket is \(V_0U\).  Therefore
\[
 V_0U(U^{\mathsf T}W^\sharp U)^{-1}U^{\mathsf T}O^{\mathsf T}
 =V_0(W^\sharp)^{-1}O^{\mathsf T},
\]
while the second term \(Z^\sharp(I_p-P^\sharp)\) is unchanged.  Hence \(\mathsf C^\sharp\), \(A^\sharp\), and \(\mu^\sharp\) are basis invariant.  Since the original representative was arbitrary and the recovered output is a basis-invariant function only of \(\eta\) and \(\Delta\), equations (3)--(23) force every admissible representative to be the same recovered triple.  This proves representative independence and exact identification of \(G(A)\), because the labels are observed and \(G(A)=\{j\to i:A_{ij}>0\}\).

Finally, the displayed witnesses satisfy every condition strictly.  For the four-dimensional example, the nonzero block is a weighted three-cycle with eigenvalues \(\varepsilon_\Delta\), \(\varepsilon_\Delta e^{2\pi i/3}\), and \(\varepsilon_\Delta e^{4\pi i/3}\).  Thus \(\rho(A)=\varepsilon_\Delta\leq1/2<\beta=1\), its rank is three, and on its image
\[
 \max_{z\in\operatorname{spec}(\Delta B|_S)}|\operatorname{Im}z|
 =\Delta\sqrt3\,\varepsilon_\Delta/2\leq\pi/4<\pi.
\]
It has a directed cycle and one isolated coordinate.  For the three-dimensional reciprocal example, the nonzero block has eigenvalues \(1/2\) and \(-1/2\), so \(\rho(A)=1/2<1\), its rank is two, and the spectrum of \(\Delta B|_S\) is real for every \(\Delta>0\).  It contains both directions of the pair and one isolated coordinate.  Both matrices are nonnegative, nonzero, and zero-diagonal, and both baseline vectors are strictly positive.  To see existence rather than merely formal parameter admissibility, use the immigration--birth construction with independent rate-one immigrants and offspring kernel \(Ae^{-u}\): the expected total progeny matrix is \(\sum_{h\geq0}A^h=(I_p-A)^{-1}\), finite because \(\rho(A)<1\), so every cluster is finite almost surely; stationary superposition over immigrants is locally finite, is simple because offspring delays have continuous densities, and has the displayed Hawkes intensity.  Hence both witnesses induce laws in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\) and their moment triples belong to \(\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{0,+,\mathrm{rs}}\).
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The theorem extends exact fixed-bin recovery to positive-rank singular feedback networks without replacing either the all-bin-width acyclic theorem or the ambient full-rank theorem. \textit{Gap.} Full-lag and full-spectrum Hawkes identification do not furnish this basis-invariant mean-plus-two-lag inverse on a singular stable-feedback image. \textit{Consumer.} Low-resolution alarm networks with singular directed cycles or reciprocal pairs can be de-aliased when the generator satisfies the restricted principal-log strip.

\begin{lemma}[lem:zero-lag-bin-covariance]\label{lem:zero-lag-bin-covariance}
For every law in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\), and hence for every law in \(\overline{\mathcal M}_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{DAG}}\),
\[
 \Gamma_0=\Delta D+\int_0^\Delta(\Delta-t)
 \{C_{+}(t)+C_{+}(t)^{\mathsf T}\}\,dt,
 \qquad m=\Delta\lambda.
\]
Consequently,
\[
 D=\Delta^{-1}\!\left[\Gamma_0-
 \int_0^\Delta(\Delta-t)
 \{C_{+}(t)+C_{+}(t)^{\mathsf T}\}\,dt\right],
 \qquad \operatorname{diag}(D)=\lambda\in(0,\infty)^p.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Fix a law in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\), and put \(I=[0,\Delta)\).  By \(\text{def:stable-common-exponential-class}\), the labeled point process is simple, has an absolutely continuous predictable compensator \(\ell(t)\,dt\), is stationary, and has finite stationary intensity \(\lambda=\mathbb E\{\ell(t)\}\).  For coordinate indices \(a,b\), define the centered covariance measure on \(I^2\) by
\[
 \mathcal V_{ab}(du,dv)
 =\mathbb E\!\left[
   \{N_a(du)-\lambda_a\,du\}
   \{N_b(dv)-\lambda_b\,dv\}
 \right].
\tag{1}
\]
Simplicity and the absolutely continuous compensator imply that the only diagonal mass in (1) is
\(\mathbf 1\{a=b\}\lambda_a\,du\,\delta_u(dv)\): indeed, on the diagonal a jump indicator squares to itself, distinct labels do not jump simultaneously in a simple labeled process, and the compensator has no atoms.  Off the diagonal, stationarity and the definition of \(C_{+}\) give the matrix-valued decomposition
\[
 \mathcal V(du,dv)
 =D\,du\,\delta_u(dv)
  +\mathbf 1\{u>v\}C_{+}(u-v)\,du\,dv
  +\mathbf 1\{v>u\}C_{+}(v-u)^{\mathsf T}\,du\,dv.
\tag{2}
\]
The density in (2) is integrable on \(I^2\): by \(\text{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}\), it equals \(e^{Bt}K\) for \(t>0\), hence is continuous on the compact interval \((0,\Delta]\) and has a finite right limit at zero.

By \(\text{def:population-moments}\), \(\Gamma_0=\mathcal V(I,I)\).  Integrating the diagonal term in (2) gives \(\Delta D\).  On the triangle \(u>v\), the change of variables \(t=u-v\) gives
\[
 \int_0^\Delta\!\int_0^u C_{+}(u-v)\,dv\,du
 =\int_0^\Delta(\Delta-t)C_{+}(t)\,dt.
\tag{3}
\]
The other triangle similarly gives the transpose of (3).  Thus (2)--(3) prove
\[
 \Gamma_0=\Delta D+\int_0^\Delta(\Delta-t)
 \{C_{+}(t)+C_{+}(t)^{\mathsf T}\}\,dt.
\tag{4}
\]
Moreover, the compensator identity and stationarity yield
\[
 m=\mathbb E\{N(\Delta)-N(0)\}
   =\mathbb E\!\int_0^\Delta\ell(u)\,du
   =\Delta\lambda.
\tag{5}
\]
Membership in the stable class gives \(\Delta>0\), so division in (4)--(5) is valid.  Nonnegative excitation and the strictly positive baseline give \(\ell_i(t)\geq\mu_i>0\) almost surely, whence \(\lambda_i\geq\mu_i>0\).  Therefore \(D=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda)\succ0\), and the displayed inverse for \(D\) follows from (4).

It remains to justify the asserted inclusion of the acyclic closure.  For a finite acyclic labeled graph, repeatedly removing a source produces a topological ordering.  In that ordering the nonnegative matrix \(A\) is strictly triangular, because a diagonal entry would be a directed cycle of length one.  Hence \(A^p=0\), so \(\rho(A/\beta)=0<1\) because \(\beta>0\).  Every law in \(\overline{\mathcal M}_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{DAG}}\) therefore satisfies \(\text{def:stable-common-exponential-class}\), and (1)--(5) apply to it as well.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The diagonal atom of the zero-lag covariance measure is the common bridge that reconstructs stationary intensity from three bin-covariance matrices. \textit{Gap.} The positive-lag semigroup alone does not account for the diagonal counting-measure atom inside a single bin. \textit{Consumer.} All three covariance-only population inverses use this identity to reconstruct the omitted mean.

\begin{theorem}[thm:full-rank-stable-covariance-only-recovery]\label{thm:full-rank-stable-covariance-only-recovery}
For every \(\gamma=(\Gamma_0,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\in\mathfrak J_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{fr}}\), every operation in \(\text{def:full-rank-covariance-only-inverse}\) is well defined and independent of the admissible structural representative.  For every such representative,
\[
 T^0=T,\quad B^0=B,\quad J^0=J,\quad K^0=K,
 \quad \widetilde C_{+}^{\mathrm{fr}}(t)=C_{+}(t)\ (0<t\leq\Delta),
 \quad D^0=D,\quad m^0=m,
\]
and
\[
 \Psi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{fr},0}(\Gamma_0,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)
 = (\beta,A,\mu).
\]
Thus three covariance matrices identify the stable full-rank principal-strip branch without a supplied mean.  The domain is strictly non-DAG: for every \(p\geq2\) and \(\Delta>0\), it contains the stationary law with \(\beta=1\), \(\mu=\mathbf 1_p\), and \(A=\tfrac12 I_p\), whose graph has a self-loop at every label.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(\gamma\in\mathfrak J_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{fr}}\).  By \(\text{def:full-rank-stable-covariance-image}\), choose an arbitrary admissible structural representative \((\beta,A,\mu)\), and let \(m\) be its population bin mean.  Then \(\eta=(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\) belongs to \(\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{fr}}\).  By \(\text{thm:full-rank-stable-two-lag-recovery}\), \(\Gamma_1\) is invertible, the principal logarithm exists, \(B\) and \(J\) are invertible, and the observable operations in the first line of \(\text{def:full-rank-covariance-only-inverse}\) obey
\[
 T^0=T,\qquad B^0=B,\qquad J^0=J,\qquad K^0=K.
\tag{1}
\]
Thus every inverse and logarithm in that line is defined.  By (1) and \(\text{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}\),
\[
 \widetilde C_{+}^{\mathrm{fr}}(t)=e^{B^0t}K^0=e^{Bt}K=C_{+}(t)
 \qquad(0<t\leq\Delta).
\tag{2}
\]
The integrand extends continuously to \(t=0\), so the finite-interval integral defining \(D^0\) exists.  Substituting (2) into \(\text{lem:zero-lag-bin-covariance}\) gives
\[
 D^0=D=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda)\succ0,\qquad
 \lambda^0=\lambda,\qquad m^0=\Delta\lambda=m.
\tag{3}
\]
Consequently \((m^0,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)=\eta\in\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{fr}}\), so the last line of the covariance-only map is total.  Equations (3) and \(\text{thm:full-rank-stable-two-lag-recovery}\) imply
\[
 \Psi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{fr},0}(\gamma)
 =\Phi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{fr}}(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)
 =(\beta,A,\mu).
\tag{4}
\]
All quantities on the left of (1)--(4) are functions only of \(\gamma\) and the known \(\Delta\).  Since the representative was arbitrary, (1)--(4) hold for every representative and force all representatives to equal the same recovered triple.  This proves representative independence.

For strictness, fix \(p\geq2\) and \(\Delta>0\), and take \(\beta=1\), \(\mu=\mathbf 1_p\), and \(A=\tfrac12 I_p\).  Then \(A\geq0\), \(\det(A)=2^{-p}\neq0\), \(\rho(A/\beta)=1/2<1\), and
\[
 \operatorname{spec}\{\Delta(A-\beta I_p)\}=\{-\Delta/2\}
 \subset\{z:|\operatorname{Im}z|<\pi\}.
\tag{5}
\]
The immigration--birth construction exists here directly: immigrants of each label form independent rate-one Poisson processes, and every event has same-label offspring with intensity \(\tfrac12e^{-u}\,du\).  The expected number of generation-\(h\) descendants is \(2^{-h}\), so the expected total cluster size is \(\sum_{h\geq0}2^{-h}=2<\infty\); hence every cluster is finite almost surely and the stationary superposition is locally finite.  Continuous offspring delays make it simple, and summing the independent offspring intensities gives the stated Hawkes conditional intensity.  Thus this parameter triple induces a law in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\), and its covariance triple belongs to \(\mathfrak J_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{fr}}\).  Since \(A_{ii}=1/2>0\), its graph has self-loop cycles and is not a DAG.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} It shows that zero-lag covariance replaces the mean in the ambient full-rank feedback inverse and proves a strict self-exciting non-DAG witness. \textit{Gap.} Complete-lag and spectral Hawkes results do not isolate this exact three-covariance fixed-bin inverse on the full-rank principal-strip image. \textit{Consumer.} Population covariance archives can identify full-rank reciprocal, cyclic, or self-exciting stable networks without retained marginal means.

\begin{theorem}[thm:restricted-rank-stable-covariance-only-recovery]\label{thm:restricted-rank-stable-covariance-only-recovery}
For every \(\gamma=(\Gamma_0,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\in
\mathfrak J_{\Delta,p}^{0,+,\mathrm{rs}}\), every operation in \(\text{def:restricted-rank-covariance-only-inverse}\) is well defined, the reconstructed curve and output are independent of the orthonormal basis \(O\), and all quantities are independent of the admissible structural representative.  For every such representative,
\[
 \widetilde C_{+}^{\mathrm{rs}}(t)=C_{+}(t)\ (0<t\leq\Delta),
 \qquad D^0=D,\qquad m^0=m,
\]
and
\[
 \Psi_{\Delta,p}^{0,+,\mathrm{rs},0}
 (\Gamma_0,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)=(\beta,A,\mu).
\]
Thus three covariance matrices identify the nonempty stable zero-diagonal restricted-strip branch without a supplied mean.  For every \(\Delta>0\), this covariance image contains both a rank-three directed cycle with an isolated fourth label and a rank-two reciprocal pair with an isolated third label, so it is strictly larger than the acyclic regime.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(\gamma\in\mathfrak J_{\Delta,p}^{0,+,\mathrm{rs}}\).  By \(\text{def:restricted-rank-stable-covariance-image}\), choose an arbitrary admissible structural representative \((\beta,A,\mu)\), and denote its population bin mean by \(m\).  Then \(\eta=(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\) belongs to \(\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{0,+,\mathrm{rs}}\).  The conclusions of \(\text{thm:restricted-rank-stable-two-lag-recovery}\), applied to this \(\eta\), show that \(r\geq1\), \(R\) has full row rank, the principal logarithm of \(F\) exists, and \(L^\sharp\) and \(F-I_r\) are invertible.  Hence all positive-lag operations in \(\text{def:restricted-rank-covariance-only-inverse}\) are defined.  That theorem also gives
\[
 L^\sharp=O^{\mathsf T}(B|_S)O,\qquad
 K=O(H^\sharp)^2R,\qquad S=\operatorname{im}(K),
\tag{1}
\]
where the first equality denotes the coordinate matrix of \(B|_S\).  Since the columns of \(K\) lie in the \(B\)-invariant space \(S\), (1) and \(\text{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}\) give
\[
 \widetilde C_{+}^{\mathrm{rs}}(t)
 =Oe^{L^\sharp t}(H^\sharp)^2R
 =e^{Bt}K=C_{+}(t),
 \qquad 0<t\leq\Delta.
\tag{2}
\]

This also proves basis invariance explicitly.  If \(O'=OU\) is another orthonormal basis of \(S\), with \(U\) orthogonal, then coordinate representations of the same operators give
\[
 R'=U^{\mathsf T}R,\qquad
 F'=U^{\mathsf T}FU,\qquad
 L'=U^{\mathsf T}L^\sharp U,\qquad
 H'=U^{\mathsf T}H^\sharp U.
\tag{3}
\]
Substitution in the reconstructed curve yields
\[
 O'e^{L't}(H')^2R'
 =OU\,U^{\mathsf T}e^{L^\sharp t}U
   U^{\mathsf T}(H^\sharp)^2U\,U^{\mathsf T}R
 =Oe^{L^\sharp t}(H^\sharp)^2R.
\tag{4}
\]
Thus \(D^0\), \(m^0\), and the input passed to the already basis-invariant map \(\Phi_{\Delta,p}^{0,+,\mathrm{rs}}\) do not depend on \(O\).

The integrand in (2) extends continuously to zero.  Applying \(\text{lem:zero-lag-bin-covariance}\) and substituting (2) gives
\[
 D^0=D=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda)\succ0,\qquad
 \lambda^0=\lambda,\qquad m^0=\Delta\lambda=m.
\tag{5}
\]
Therefore \((m^0,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)=\eta\) belongs to the mean-plus-two-lag image domain, so the final operation is total.  By \(\text{thm:restricted-rank-stable-two-lag-recovery}\),
\[
 \Psi_{\Delta,p}^{0,+,\mathrm{rs},0}(\gamma)
 =\Phi_{\Delta,p}^{0,+,\mathrm{rs}}(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)
 =(\beta,A,\mu).
\tag{6}
\]
The left side of (2), (5), and (6) is a function only of \(\gamma\), the known \(\Delta\), and an inessential basis; since the representative was arbitrary, these equations prove representative independence and uniqueness of the recovered structural triple.

For the promised strict witnesses, let
\[
 \varepsilon_\Delta=\min\{1/2,\pi/(2\sqrt3\Delta)\},\qquad
 A_{\mathrm{cyc}}=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 0&0&\varepsilon_\Delta&0\\
 \varepsilon_\Delta&0&0&0\\
 0&\varepsilon_\Delta&0&0\\
 0&0&0&0
 \end{pmatrix},\qquad
 A_{\mathrm{rec}}=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 0&1/2&0\\
 1/2&0&0\\
 0&0&0
 \end{pmatrix}.
\tag{7}
\]
Take \(\beta=1\) and respectively \(\mu=\mathbf 1_4\) and \(\mu=\mathbf 1_3\).  As proved in \(\text{thm:restricted-rank-stable-two-lag-recovery}\), the first law is stable of rank three, its restricted imaginary parts are bounded by \(\pi/4\), and it has a directed three-cycle plus an isolated label; the second is stable of rank two, has real restricted spectrum, and has a reciprocal pair plus an isolated label.  Both are nonzero, nonnegative, and zero-diagonal, and the cited theorem's immigration--birth verification supplies stationary laws.  Their covariance triples therefore satisfy \(\text{def:restricted-rank-stable-covariance-image}\), proving the final assertion.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} It establishes a basis-invariant covariance-only inverse on the singular zero-diagonal feedback image and certifies strict cycle and reciprocal witnesses. \textit{Gap.} Existing second-order Hawkes identification does not provide this exact restricted-image three-covariance de-aliasing map for singular feedback networks. \textit{Consumer.} Covariance-only alarm summaries can recover singular cyclic or reciprocal stable networks when the restricted principal-log condition holds.

\begin{theorem}[thm:isolated-invertible-block-two-lag-recovery]\label{thm:isolated-invertible-block-two-lag-recovery}
For every \(\eta=(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\in\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{ib}}\), every operation in \(\text{def:isolated-invertible-block-two-lag-inverse}\) is well defined.  If \((\beta,A,\mu,I,E_I,A_I)\) is any structural representative in \(\text{def:isolated-invertible-block-stable-moment-image}\), then
\[
 S=\operatorname{im}(\Gamma_1)=\operatorname{im}(A)
   =\operatorname{span}\{e_i:i\in I\},\qquad
 I=\{i:e_i\in S\},\qquad r=|I|=\operatorname{rank}(\Gamma_1),
\]
so the active label set and its ordered selector are unique.  Writing
\[
 L_I=A_I-\beta I_r,\quad
 J_I=\int_0^\Delta e^{L_Iu}\,du,\quad
 D_I=E_I^{\mathsf T}DE_I,\quad
 Q_I=\int_0^\infty e^{L_Iu}D_Ie^{L_I^{\mathsf T}u}\,du,\quad
 K_I=A_I(2\beta I_r-A_I)Q_I,
\]
the recovered quantities satisfy
\[
 F^\circ=e^{L_I\Delta},\quad L^\circ=L_I,\quad J^\circ=J_I,\quad
 K^\circ=K_I=(\beta^2I_r-L_I^2)Q_I,\quad Q^\circ=Q_I\succ0,\quad s^\circ=\beta^2>0,
\]
and
\[
 \Phi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{ib}}(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)=(\beta,A,\mu).
\]
The output is unchanged if the active space is represented by any orthonormal basis rather than the canonical ordered selector, and it is independent of the admissible structural representative.  For every \(\Delta>0\), the domain contains the law with \(p=2\), \(\beta=1\), \(A=\operatorname{diag}(1/2,0)\), and \(\mu=\mathbf 1_2\), and it also contains the law with \(p=3\), \(\beta=1\), \(\mu=\mathbf 1_3\), and
\[
 A=\begin{pmatrix}1/4&1/4&0\\1/4&1/2&0\\0&0&0\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Thus this singular branch includes self-excitation and, in the second witness, an invertible reciprocal self-exciting active block, while retaining an isolated observed label.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(\eta\in\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{ib}}\), and choose an arbitrary representative \((\beta,A,\mu,I,E_I,A_I)\) supplied by \(\text{def:isolated-invertible-block-stable-moment-image}\).  Put \(r=|I|\), \(L_I=A_I-\beta I_r\), and let \(E_0\) be the ordered selector of the complementary labels.  Since \([E_I\ E_0]\) is a permutation matrix, the identities \(A=E_IA_IE_I^{\mathsf T}\) and \(D=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda)\) give, in these coordinates,
\[
 A=\begin{pmatrix}A_I&0\\0&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 B=\begin{pmatrix}L_I&0\\0&-\beta I_{p-r}\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 D=\begin{pmatrix}D_I&0\\0&D_0\end{pmatrix},
\tag{1}
\]
where \(D_I=E_I^{\mathsf T}DE_I\succ0\) and \(D_0=E_0^{\mathsf T}DE_0\succ0\).  Positivity follows because every stationary intensity coordinate is strictly positive in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\).

The restriction \(\rho(A_I)<\beta\) implies that every eigenvalue of \(L_I\) has negative real part; thus \(L_I\) is Hurwitz and invertible.  The block form (1) and the definition of \(Q\) yield
\[
 Q=E_IQ_IE_I^{\mathsf T}+E_0Q_0E_0^{\mathsf T},\qquad
 Q_I=\int_0^\infty e^{L_Iu}D_Ie^{L_I^{\mathsf T}u}\,du,\qquad
 Q_0=(2\beta)^{-1}D_0.
\tag{2}
\]
The integral for \(Q_I\) converges because \(L_I\) is Hurwitz.  For every nonzero \(x\in\mathbb R^r\), its integrand is continuous and at \(u=0\) equals \(x^{\mathsf T}D_Ix>0\); hence \(x^{\mathsf T}Q_Ix>0\), proving \(Q_I\succ0\).

By \(\text{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}\) and (1)--(2),
\[
 K=E_IK_IE_I^{\mathsf T},\qquad
 K_I=A_I(2\beta I_r-A_I)Q_I.
\tag{3}
\]
The matrices \(A_I\) and \(Q_I\) are invertible by the image definition and (2).  If \(z\in\operatorname{spec}(A_I)\), then \(|z|\leq\rho(A_I)<\beta\), so \(2\beta-z\neq0\); hence \(2\beta I_r-A_I\) is invertible.  Equation (3) therefore makes \(K_I\) invertible.

Let
\[
 J_I=\int_0^\Delta e^{L_Iu}\,du=L_I^{-1}(e^{L_I\Delta}-I_r).
\tag{4}
\]
Every eigenvalue of \(e^{L_I\Delta}\) has modulus strictly less than one, so \(e^{L_I\Delta}-I_r\) and hence \(J_I\) are invertible.  The same block calculation applied to \(J\), followed by the covariance identity in \(\text{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}\), gives
\[
 \Gamma_1=E_IJ_I^2K_IE_I^{\mathsf T},\qquad
 \Gamma_2=E_Ie^{L_I\Delta}J_I^2K_IE_I^{\mathsf T}.
\tag{5}
\]
Because \(J_I^2K_I\) is invertible, (5) implies
\[
 S=\operatorname{im}(\Gamma_1)=\operatorname{im}(E_I)
 =\operatorname{im}(A)=\operatorname{span}\{e_i:i\in I\},\qquad
 \operatorname{rank}(\Gamma_1)=r.
\tag{6}
\]
Consequently \(I=\{i:e_i\in S\}\).  This set is determined by \(\Gamma_1\), and ordering its labels increasingly makes \(E_I\) unique.  In particular, two structural representatives of the same moment triple must use the same active set and ordered selector.

Put \(H_I=J_I^2K_I\).  From (5), \(\Gamma_1=E_IH_IE_I^{\mathsf T}\) with \(H_I\) invertible.  Direct verification of the four Moore--Penrose equations gives
\[
 \Gamma_1^\dagger=E_IH_I^{-1}E_I^{\mathsf T}.
\tag{7}
\]
Equations (5)--(7) now give the observable restricted transition
\[
 F^\circ=E_I^{\mathsf T}\Gamma_2\Gamma_1^\dagger E_I
 =e^{L_I\Delta}H_IH_I^{-1}=e^{L_I\Delta}.
\tag{8}
\]
The image condition places \(\operatorname{spec}(\Delta L_I)\) in the open principal strip.  Thus \(\text{lem:principal-log-strip}\) applied to (8) proves that the principal logarithm exists and
\[
 L^\circ=\Delta^{-1}\operatorname{Log}(F^\circ)=L_I.
\tag{9}
\]
Equations (4), (5), and (9) show that \(J^\circ=J_I\) is invertible and, crucially,
\[
 K^\circ=(J^\circ)^{-2}E_I^{\mathsf T}\Gamma_1E_I
 =J_I^{-2}J_I^2K_I=K_I.
\tag{10}
\]
Thus the two-lag inverse is total.  Notice that (10) uses two left factors of \(J_I^{-1}\); a congruence with \(J_I^{-\mathsf T}\) would not follow from \(\Gamma_1=J^2K\) and would generally fail when \(J_I\) is nonsymmetric.

Stationarity and \(\text{def:population-moments}\) give \(m=\Delta\lambda\), so \(\lambda^\circ=\lambda\) and \(D_I^\circ=D_I\).  Combining this with (2) and (9) proves
\[
 Q^\circ=Q_I\succ0.
\tag{11}
\]
It is also the unique solution of \(L_IQ+QL_I^{\mathsf T}=-D_I\): differentiating the integrand in (2) proves the equation, while the difference \(H\) of two solutions satisfies \(e^{L_Iu}He^{L_I^{\mathsf T}u}=H\) and hence is zero after letting \(u\to\infty\).

Since \(A_I=L_I+\beta I_r\), (3) becomes
\[
 K_I=(L_I+\beta I_r)(\beta I_r-L_I)Q_I
     =(\beta^2I_r-L_I^2)Q_I.
\tag{12}
\]
Equations (9)--(12) imply
\[
 K^\circ(Q^\circ)^{-1}+(L^\circ)^2=\beta^2I_r,\qquad
 s^\circ=r^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\beta^2I_r)=\beta^2>0.
\tag{13}
\]
Because \(r\geq1\), division by \(r\) is valid; because \(\beta>0\), the positive square root in the definition gives \(\beta^\circ=\beta\).  It follows from (6), (9), and (13) that
\[
 A^\circ=E_I(L_I+\beta I_r)E_I^{\mathsf T}=E_IA_IE_I^{\mathsf T}=A.
\tag{14}
\]
Taking expectations in the nonnegative Hawkes intensity, using monotone convergence, stationarity, and \(\beta>0\), gives
\[
 \lambda=\mu+\int_0^\infty Ae^{-\beta u}\lambda\,du
 =\mu+(A/\beta)\lambda.
\tag{15}
\]
Therefore (14)--(15) yield \(\mu^\circ=(I_p-A/\beta)\lambda=\mu\).

For basis invariance, let \(O=E_IU\), where \(U\in\mathbb R^{r\times r}\) is orthogonal, and perform the same construction with \(O\) in place of \(E_I\).  Similarity covariance of the principal logarithm and direct substitution give
\[
 F_O=U^{\mathsf T}F^\circ U,\quad L_O=U^{\mathsf T}L_IU,\quad
 J_O=U^{\mathsf T}J_IU,\quad K_O=U^{\mathsf T}K_IU,\quad
 D_O=U^{\mathsf T}D_IU,\quad Q_O=U^{\mathsf T}Q_IU.
\tag{16}
\]
The trace in (13) is similarity invariant, and
\[
 O(L_O+\beta I_r)O^{\mathsf T}=E_I(L_I+\beta I_r)E_I^{\mathsf T}.
\tag{17}
\]
Thus the recovered scalar and ambient matrices do not depend on active-space coordinates.  Since all recovered quantities are functions only of \(\eta\), \(\Delta\), and an inessential coordinate choice, while the initial representative was arbitrary, equations (6)--(17) also prove representative independence.

It remains to verify that the displayed strict witnesses really lie in the image.  For the first, take \(p=2\), \(I=\{1\}\), \(A_I=(1/2)\), \(\beta=1\), and \(\mu=\mathbf1_2\).  Then \(A=\operatorname{diag}(1/2,0)\), \(A_I\) is nonnegative and invertible, \(\rho(A_I)=1/2<1\), and \(\operatorname{spec}\{\Delta(A_I-I_1)\}=\{-\Delta/2\}\) is real for every \(\Delta>0\).  For the second, take \(p=3\), \(I=\{1,2\}\), \(\beta=1\), \(\mu=\mathbf1_3\), and
\[
 A_I=\begin{pmatrix}1/4&1/4\\1/4&1/2\end{pmatrix}.
\tag{18}
\]
This matrix is entrywise positive, has determinant \(1/16\), and has eigenvalues \((3-\sqrt5)/8\) and \((3+\sqrt5)/8\).  Both are real and the larger is less than one, so the stability and principal-strip conditions hold strictly for every \(\Delta>0\).  It has two positive diagonal entries and both reciprocal off-diagonal entries, while the third label is isolated from excitation.

For completeness, both parameter triples induce stationary simple Hawkes laws.  In the immigration--birth construction, label-\(i\) immigrants form independent Poisson processes of rate \(\mu_i\), and a label-\(j\) event has label-\(i\) children with Poisson intensity \(A_{ij}e^{-u}\,du\).  The mean offspring matrix is \(A/\beta\).  In both witnesses its spectral radius is below one, so the entrywise nonnegative Neumann series \(\sum_{h\geq0}(A/\beta)^h\) is finite.  Hence the expected total size of each immigrant cluster is finite, which implies that every cluster is finite almost surely.  The stationary superposition over immigrants has finite expected count on every bounded interval and is therefore locally finite almost surely; continuous offspring delays make simultaneous points a null event, and summing the offspring hazards gives the stated Hawkes conditional intensity.  Thus these are laws in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\), and their moment triples belong to \(\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{ib}}\).
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This theorem adds an exact singular-feedback branch in which the active image is a proper coordinate subspace and the active excitation block is invertible, allowing self-loops and reciprocal excitation while identifying the active labels themselves. \textit{Gap.} The acyclic theorem handles arbitrary singular feed-forward matrices, the zero-diagonal restricted theorem handles singular feedback without self-loops, and the full-rank theorem handles self-loops without isolated labels; none of those statements covers an invertible self-exciting active block embedded among isolated observed labels. \textit{Consumer.} A cellular-alarm panel may contain an interacting reciprocal subsystem together with observed alarm labels that have positive exogenous rates but no excitation links.

\begin{theorem}[thm:isolated-invertible-block-covariance-only-recovery]\label{thm:isolated-invertible-block-covariance-only-recovery}
For every \(\gamma=(\Gamma_0,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\in\mathfrak J_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{ib}}\), every operation in \(\text{def:isolated-invertible-block-covariance-only-inverse}\) is well defined, the active label set is uniquely determined by \(S=\operatorname{im}(\Gamma_1)\), and the recovered curve and output are independent of active-space coordinates and of the admissible structural representative.  For every such representative,
\[
 \widetilde C_{+}^{\mathrm{ib}}(t)=C_{+}(t)\quad(0<t\leq\Delta),\qquad
 D^0=D,\qquad \lambda^0=\lambda,\qquad m^0=m,
\]
and
\[
 \Psi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{ib},0}(\Gamma_0,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)=(\beta,A,\mu).
\]
Consequently three covariance matrices identify the isolated invertible-block principal-strip branch without a supplied mean.  Its covariance image contains the covariance triples of both strict witnesses in \(\text{thm:isolated-invertible-block-two-lag-recovery}\).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(\gamma\in\mathfrak J_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{ib}}\), choose an arbitrary admissible structural representative \((\beta,A,\mu,I,E_I,A_I)\), and let \(m\) be its population bin mean.  By the two image definitions,
\[
 (m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\in\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{ib}}.
\tag{1}
\]
Applying \(\text{thm:isolated-invertible-block-two-lag-recovery}\) to (1) proves that \(S=\operatorname{im}(\Gamma_1)\) uniquely determines \(I\) and its ordered selector, and that every inverse and logarithm in the positive-lag part of \(\text{def:isolated-invertible-block-covariance-only-inverse}\) exists.  It also gives
\[
 L^0=L_I=A_I-\beta I_r,\qquad J^0=J_I,\qquad K^0=K_I.
\tag{2}
\]
The block calculations (1)--(3) in the proof of that theorem, together with \(\text{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}\), show that \(K=E_IK_IE_I^{\mathsf T}\) and that the active subspace is invariant under \(B\).  Hence, for \(t>0\),
\[
 \widetilde C_{+}^{\mathrm{ib}}(t)
 =E_Ie^{L^0t}K^0E_I^{\mathsf T}
 =e^{Bt}K=C_{+}(t).
\tag{3}
\]
This curve extends continuously to \(t=0\), so the integral in the covariance-only definition is finite.  Substitution of (3) into \(\text{lem:zero-lag-bin-covariance}\) yields
\[
 D^0=\Delta^{-1}\!\left[\Gamma_0-
 \int_0^\Delta(\Delta-t)\{C_{+}(t)+C_{+}(t)^{\mathsf T}\}\,dt\right]
 =D,
\tag{4}
\]
and therefore \(\lambda^0=\operatorname{diag}(D)=\lambda\) and \(m^0=\Delta\lambda=m\).  In particular, the triple passed to \(\Phi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{ib}}\) is exactly the triple in (1), so
\[
 \Psi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{ib},0}(\gamma)
 =\Phi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{ib}}(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)
 =(\beta,A,\mu)
\tag{5}
\]
by \(\text{thm:isolated-invertible-block-two-lag-recovery}\).  Equations (2)--(5), and the basis-invariance part of that theorem, prove totality, coordinate invariance, and representative independence.  Finally, each witness law proved there has a zero-lag covariance as well as two positive-lag covariances and satisfies the identical structural restrictions in \(\text{def:isolated-invertible-block-stable-covariance-image}\); its covariance triple therefore belongs to \(\mathfrak J_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{ib}}\).
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The zero-lag covariance atom recovers all innovation intensities, including those of labels isolated from excitation, and makes the proper-subset active-block inverse mean-free. \textit{Gap.} Existing covariance-only branches do not cover a singular ambient excitation matrix whose proper active block is invertible and may contain both reciprocal edges and self-loops. \textit{Consumer.} Covariance archives of coarse cellular alarms can recover an interacting active subsystem and the baselines of isolated labels even when marginal count means were not retained.

\begin{lemma}[lem:bacry-muzy-wiener-hopf-uniqueness]\label{lem:bacry-muzy-wiener-hopf-uniqueness}
Let \(N\) be a stationary \(p\)-dimensional linear Hawkes process with positive causal kernel matrix \(\phi\), suppose every entry of \(\phi\) is in \(L^1(\mathbb R_+)\), and suppose the spectral radius of the matrix \((\int_0^\infty\phi^{ij}(t)\,dt)_{i,j}\) is strictly less than one.  Define its nonsingular conditional-response density by
\[
 g^{ij}(t)\,dt
 =\mathbb E\{dN_i(t)\mid dN_j(0)=1\}
  -\mathbf 1_{\{i=j\}}\delta_0(dt)-\lambda_i\,dt.
\]
Then \(\phi\) is the unique matrix function \(\chi\) whose entries are causal and in \(L^1(\mathbb R_+)\) and which solves
\[
 g(t)=\chi(t)+(\chi*g)(t),\qquad t>0,
\]
where \((\chi*g)(t)=\int_0^\infty\chi(s)g(t-s)\,ds\) with matrix multiplication.
\end{lemma}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This is the published uniqueness theorem that converts the reconstructed complete conditional response into the Hawkes kernel. \textit{Gap.} BacryMuzy2016 assumes the complete response is available and does not reconstruct it from two fixed-bin covariance lags. \textit{Consumer.} The broad stable nonzero recovery theorem uses precisely this uniqueness step after completing the response from the two observed lags.

\begin{lemma}[lem:complete-response-orientation]\label{lem:complete-response-orientation}
For every law in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\), the nonsingular conditional-response density in \(\text{lem:bacry-muzy-wiener-hopf-uniqueness}\) agrees almost everywhere with \(g\) in \(\text{def:complete-hawkes-response}\).  Explicitly,
\[
 C_{+}(t)_{ij}=g^{ij}(t)\lambda_j\quad(t>0),
\]
and consequently
\[
 g(t)=C_{+}(t)D^{-1}\quad(t>0),\qquad
 g(t)=C_{+}(-t)^{\mathsf T}D^{-1}\quad(t<0).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Fix a law in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\).  Equation (2) in the proof of \(\text{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}\) gives \(\lambda_j>0\) for every \(j\), so conditioning on an infinitesimal type-\(j\) event is well normalized.  Let \(\bar g\) denote the nonsingular conditional-response density used in \(\text{lem:bacry-muzy-wiener-hopf-uniqueness}\).  For \(t>0\), there is no diagonal Dirac atom between times zero and \(t\).  Stationarity and \(\mathbb P\{dN_j(0)=1\}=\lambda_j\,dt+o(dt)\) therefore give, entry by entry,
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 C_{+}(t)_{ij}\,dt^2
 &=\mathbb E\{dN_i(t)dN_j(0)\}-\lambda_i\lambda_j\,dt^2\\
 &=\lambda_j\,dt\left[
   \mathbb E\{dN_i(t)\mid dN_j(0)=1\}-\lambda_i\,dt
  \right]
 =\lambda_j\bar g^{ij}(t)\,dt^2.
 \end{aligned}
\tag{1}
\]
Thus \(C_{+}(t)=\bar g(t)D\), and right multiplication by \(D^{-1}\) is valid because \(D\succ0\).  Hence
\[
 \bar g(t)=C_{+}(t)D^{-1},\qquad t>0.
\tag{2}
\]
For \(t<0\), covariance symmetry and stationarity give
\[
 \operatorname{Cov}\{dN(t),dN(0)\}
 =C_{+}(-t)^{\mathsf T}\,dt^2.
\tag{3}
\]
Repeating (1) with the negative lag and retaining the time-zero coordinate in the conditioning column yields
\[
 \bar g(t)=C_{+}(-t)^{\mathsf T}D^{-1},\qquad t<0.
\tag{4}
\]
Equations (2)--(4) are exactly the two branches in \(\text{def:complete-hawkes-response}\).  Values at \(t=0\) are irrelevant to the entrywise-\(L^1\) equivalence class and to convolution, proving the claim.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} It audits the transpose and right-normalization needed to apply the published Wiener--Hopf theorem in this note's future-row covariance convention. \textit{Gap.} BacryMuzy2016 writes the covariance with the earlier coordinate in the row, whereas this note writes the future coordinate in the row. \textit{Consumer.} The broad recovery theorem uses this bridge to pass its reconstructed covariance density to the cited conditional-response uniqueness theorem.

\begin{theorem}[thm:stable-nonzero-two-lag-wiener-hopf-recovery]\label{thm:stable-nonzero-two-lag-wiener-hopf-recovery}
For every \(\eta=(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\in\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{WH}}\), every operation in \(\text{def:wiener-hopf-two-lag-inverse}\) is well defined, independent of the orthonormal basis \(O\), and independent of the admissible structural representative.  For every such representative \((\beta,A,\mu)\),
\[
 r=\operatorname{rank}(A)\geq1,\quad S=\operatorname{im}(A),\quad
 F=[T|_S]_O,\quad L^{\mathrm{WH}}=[B|_S]_O,\quad
 H^{\mathrm{WH}}=[J|_S]_O^{-1},
\]
\[
 K^{\mathrm{WH}}=K,\quad C_{+}^{\mathrm{WH}}(t)=C_{+}(t)\quad(t>0),\quad
 D^{\mathrm{WH}}=D,\quad g^{\mathrm{WH}}=g.
\]
Writing \(P^{\mathrm{WH}}=(H^{\mathrm{WH}})^2R\), the response transform is finite dimensional:
\[
 \mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(x)
 =(P^{\mathrm{WH}})^{\mathsf T}(xI_r-(L^{\mathrm{WH}})^{\mathsf T})^{-1}O^{\mathsf T},
 \qquad x>0.
\]
Moreover,
\[
 \mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(\beta)
 =K^{\mathsf T}(2\beta I_p-A^{\mathsf T})^{-1}=QA^{\mathsf T},
\]
\[
 D+\mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(\beta)=(2\beta I_p-A)Q,
 \qquad A[D+\mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(\beta)]=K,
 \qquad AO=O(L^{\mathrm{WH}}+\beta I_r).
\]
The pair \((\beta,A)\) is the unique \((x,M)\in(0,\infty)\times\mathbb R^{p\times p}\) satisfying
\[
 MO=O(L^{\mathrm{WH}}+xI_r),\qquad
 M[D+\mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(x)]=K.
\]
Consequently \(\beta\in\Omega^{\mathrm{WH}}\), the scalar equation
\[
 \mathcal A^{\mathrm{WH}}(x)O=O(L^{\mathrm{WH}}+xI_r),\qquad x\in\Omega^{\mathrm{WH}},
\]
has the unique solution \(\beta^{\#}=\beta\), and \(A^{\#}=A\), \(\mu^{\#}=\mu\).  Hence
\[
 \Phi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{WH}}(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)=(\beta,A,\mu).
\]
Thus the mean and two positive fixed-bin covariance lags identify the entire stable nonzero common-exponential Hawkes mechanism, and hence its labeled graph, whenever the principal-log strip holds only on \(S=\operatorname{im}(A)\); no full-rank, zero-diagonal, coordinate-support, isolated-label, acyclicity, or invertible-active-block condition is required.  The inverse consists of finite-dimensional matrix operations and one uniquely characterized positive scalar root.  The regime is strictly broader than each such algebraic special case: for \(p=2\), every \(\Delta>0\), \(\beta=1\), \(\mu=\mathbf 1_2\), and \(A=\tfrac14\mathbf 1_2\mathbf 1_2^{\mathsf T}\) define an admissible member whose \(A\) is singular, self-exciting, reciprocal, and has the non-coordinate image \(\operatorname{span}(\mathbf 1_2)\).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(\eta\in\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{WH}}\) and an arbitrary admissible representative \((\beta,A,\mu)\).  By \(\text{def:stable-nonzero-restricted-strip-moment-image}\),
\[
 A\neq0,\qquad \rho(A/\beta)<1,\qquad
 \operatorname{spec}\!\left(\left.\Delta B\right|_{\operatorname{im}(A)}\right)
 \subset\{z\in\mathbb C:|\operatorname{Im}z|<\pi\}.
\tag{1}
\]
The positive decay, positive baseline, nonnegative excitation, stationarity, and stability conditions used below are all part of membership in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\).  In particular, \(\lambda\in(0,\infty)^p\), so \(D=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda)\succ0\).

By \(\text{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}\),
\[
 S=\operatorname{im}(\Gamma_1)=\operatorname{im}(A)=\operatorname{im}(K),\quad
 r=\operatorname{rank}(A)\geq1,\quad
 \Gamma_1=J^2K,\quad \Gamma_2=T\Gamma_1,
\tag{2}
\]
and \(B\) is Hurwitz while \(J\) is invertible.  The inequality \(r\geq1\) follows from \(A\neq0\).  Since \(A\operatorname{im}(A)\subseteq\operatorname{im}(A)\), the space \(S\) is invariant under \(A\), hence under \(B=A-\beta I_p\), \(T=e^{B\Delta}\), and \(J=\int_0^\Delta e^{Bu}\,du\).

Choose an admissible orthonormal basis \(O\), and put \(R=O^{\mathsf T}\Gamma_1\).  Then \(\Gamma_1=OR\).  Since \(O\) and \(\Gamma_1\) both have rank \(r\), \(R\) has full row rank and \(RR^{\mathsf T}\succ0\).  There is a unique \(F_0\) with \(TO=OF_0\).  Equation (2) yields
\[
 O^{\mathsf T}\Gamma_2R^{\mathsf T}(RR^{\mathsf T})^{-1}
 =F_0RR^{\mathsf T}(RR^{\mathsf T})^{-1}=F_0,
\tag{3}
\]
so \(F=F_0=[T|_S]_O\).  If \(L_0=O^{\mathsf T}BO\), invariance gives \(BO=OL_0\) and hence \(F=e^{\Delta L_0}\).  The strip condition in (1) and \(\text{lem:principal-log-strip}\), applied on the \(r\)-dimensional invariant space, imply
\[
 L^{\mathrm{WH}}=\Delta^{-1}\operatorname{Log}(F)=L_0=[B|_S]_O.
\tag{4}
\]
Because \(B|_S\) is Hurwitz, \(L^{\mathrm{WH}}\) is invertible and every eigenvalue of \(F\) has modulus less than one; thus \(F-I_r\) is invertible.  Moreover, \(F=e^{\Delta L^{\mathrm{WH}}}\), so \(F-I_r\) commutes with \(L^{\mathrm{WH}}\).  Writing \(J_S=O^{\mathsf T}JO\), integration of \(e^{Bu}O=Oe^{L_0u}\) gives
\[
 J_S=\int_0^\Delta e^{L_0u}\,du
 =L_0^{-1}(F-I_r),\qquad
 H^{\mathrm{WH}}=L^{\mathrm{WH}}(F-I_r)^{-1}=J_S^{-1}.
\tag{5}
\]
Write \(K=OK_S\), which is possible by (2).  Since \(J^2O=OJ_S^2\), equations (2) and (5) give
\[
 R=O^{\mathsf T}\Gamma_1=J_S^2K_S,\qquad
 P^{\mathrm{WH}}=(H^{\mathrm{WH}})^2R=K_S,\qquad
 K^{\mathrm{WH}}=OP^{\mathrm{WH}}=K.
\tag{6}
\]
Therefore, for every \(t>0\),
\[
 C_{+}^{\mathrm{WH}}(t)=Oe^{L^{\mathrm{WH}}t}P^{\mathrm{WH}}
 =e^{Bt}K=C_{+}(t)
\tag{7}
\]
by \(\text{lem:stable-covariance-semigroup}\).  This proves exact recovery of the entire positive-lag covariance density from \((\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\).

Stationarity and the compensator identity give
\[
 m=\mathbb E\{N(\Delta)-N(0)\}
 =\mathbb E\int_0^\Delta\ell(u)\,du=\Delta\lambda.
\tag{8}
\]
Because \(\Delta>0\), \(\lambda^{\mathrm{WH}}=m/\Delta=\lambda\) and \(D^{\mathrm{WH}}=D\succ0\), so all displayed inverses involving \(D^{\mathrm{WH}}\) exist.  Equations (7), (8), \(\text{def:complete-hawkes-response}\), and \(\text{lem:complete-response-orientation}\) show that \(g^{\mathrm{WH}}=g\) almost everywhere on \(\mathbb R\), including the transpose and right normalization on negative lags.

We next verify the advertised finite transform.  For \(x>0\), all eigenvalues of \((L^{\mathrm{WH}})^{\mathsf T}-xI_r\) have negative real part.  Hence its exponential tends to zero, and the fundamental theorem of calculus gives
\[
 \int_0^\infty e^{((L^{\mathrm{WH}})^{\mathsf T}-xI_r)t}\,dt
