 =(xI_r-(L^{\mathrm{WH}})^{\mathsf T})^{-1}.
\tag{9}
\]
Transposing (7) and using (9) yields
\[
 \mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(x)
 =\int_0^\infty e^{-xt}(P^{\mathrm{WH}})^{\mathsf T}
 e^{(L^{\mathrm{WH}})^{\mathsf T}t}O^{\mathsf T}\,dt
 =(P^{\mathrm{WH}})^{\mathsf T}(xI_r-(L^{\mathrm{WH}})^{\mathsf T})^{-1}O^{\mathsf T}.
\tag{10}
\]
Thus evaluating \(\mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}\) requires only an \(r\)-dimensional matrix resolvent.

We prove existence of the true root-pair.  From (7), \(B=A-\beta I_p\), and the Hurwitz property,
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(\beta)
 &=\int_0^\infty e^{-\beta t}K^{\mathsf T}e^{B^{\mathsf T}t}\,dt\\
 &=K^{\mathsf T}(2\beta I_p-A^{\mathsf T})^{-1}.
 \end{aligned}
\tag{11}
\]
The matrix \(2\beta I_p-A^{\mathsf T}\) is invertible because \(\rho(A/\beta)<1\).  Since \(Q\) is symmetric and \(K=A(2\beta I_p-A)Q\), while \(A^{\mathsf T}\) commutes with \(2\beta I_p-A^{\mathsf T}\), equation (11) becomes
\[
 \mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(\beta)
 =Q(2\beta I_p-A^{\mathsf T})A^{\mathsf T}
   (2\beta I_p-A^{\mathsf T})^{-1}=QA^{\mathsf T}.
\tag{12}
\]
For completeness, differentiate the integrand defining \(Q\).  Since \(B\) is Hurwitz, its boundary value at infinity is zero, and therefore
\[
 BQ+QB^{\mathsf T}
 =\int_0^\infty\frac{d}{du}
   \{e^{Bu}De^{B^{\mathsf T}u}\}\,du=-D.
\tag{13}
\]
Substitution of \(B=A-\beta I_p\) into (13) gives
\[
 D+QA^{\mathsf T}=(2\beta I_p-A)Q.
\tag{14}
\]
Equations (12)--(14) and the definition of \(K\) imply
\[
 D+\mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(\beta)=(2\beta I_p-A)Q,\qquad
 A[D+\mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(\beta)]=K.
\tag{15}
\]
Both \(Q\) and \(2\beta I_p-A\) are invertible, the first because \(Q\succ0\) and the second by stability.  Thus \(\beta\in\Omega^{\mathrm{WH}}\).  Finally, (4) and \(B=A-\beta I_p\) give
\[
 AO=O(L^{\mathrm{WH}}+\beta I_r).
\tag{16}
\]
Hence \((\beta,A)\) satisfies both candidate equations.

It remains to prove uniqueness without leaving an infinite-dimensional operation in the evaluator.  Let \((x,M)\in(0,\infty)\times\mathbb R^{p\times p}\) satisfy
\[
 MO=O(L^{\mathrm{WH}}+xI_r),\qquad
 M[D+\mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(x)]=K.
\tag{17}
\]
Define only for this proof the causal, entrywise-integrable comparison kernel
\[
 \chi_x(s)=Me^{-xs}\mathbf 1_{\{s>0\}}.
\tag{18}
\]
Fix \(t>0\), and split \((\chi_x*g)(t)\) at \(s=t\).  On \(0<s<t\), equations (6)--(7), the first equation in (17), and \(BO=OL^{\mathrm{WH}}\) imply that \(M\) acts as \(B+xI_p\) on every column of \(e^{B(t-s)}K\).  Therefore
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \int_0^t Me^{-xs}g(t-s)\,ds
 &=\int_0^t Me^{-xs}e^{B(t-s)}K D^{-1}\,ds\\
 &=-\int_0^t\frac{d}{ds}
   \{e^{-xs}e^{B(t-s)}K\}\,ds\,D^{-1}\\
 &=g(t)-e^{-xt}KD^{-1}.
 \end{aligned}
\tag{19}
\]
On \(s>t\), the negative-lag branch of \(g\) from \(\text{def:complete-hawkes-response}\) gives
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \int_t^\infty Me^{-xs}g(t-s)\,ds
 &=\int_t^\infty Me^{-xs}K^{\mathsf T}e^{B^{\mathsf T}(s-t)}D^{-1}\,ds\\
 &=e^{-xt}M\mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(x)D^{-1}.
 \end{aligned}
\tag{20}
\]
Adding (18)--(20), and then using the second equation in (17), proves
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \chi_x(t)+(\chi_x*g)(t)
 &=g(t)+e^{-xt}\{M+M\mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(x)D^{-1}-KD^{-1}\}\\
 &=g(t)+e^{-xt}\{M[D+\mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}(x)]-K\}D^{-1}=g(t).
 \end{aligned}
\tag{21}
\]
Thus every finite-dimensional candidate pair produces a causal entrywise-\(L^1\) solution of the original complete-response Wiener--Hopf equation.

The true Hawkes kernel \(\phi(t)=Ae^{-\beta t}\mathbf 1_{\{t>0\}}\) is causal and entrywise integrable because \(\beta>0\), and its integrated kernel is \(A/\beta\), whose spectral radius is strictly below one by (1).  By \(\text{lem:complete-response-orientation}\) and \(\text{lem:bacry-muzy-wiener-hopf-uniqueness}\), (21) forces
\[
 Me^{-xt}=Ae^{-\beta t}\quad\text{for almost every }t>0.
\tag{22}
\]
Both sides are continuous on \((0,\infty)\), so (22) holds everywhere there.  Letting \(t\downarrow0\) gives \(M=A\).  Since \(A\neq0\), some entry of \(A\) is nonzero; equality of that entry at any two distinct positive times then gives \(x=\beta\).  Thus \((\beta,A)\) is the unique candidate pair.  On \(\Omega^{\mathrm{WH}}\), the second equation in (17) is equivalent to \(M=\mathcal A^{\mathrm{WH}}(x)\), so the scalar equation in the definition has the unique root \(\beta^{\#}=\beta\), and \(A^{\#}=A\).

Taking expectations in the Hawkes intensity equation and using stationarity yields
\[
 \lambda=\mu+(A/\beta)\lambda.
\tag{23}
\]
Together with (8), (23) proves
\[
 \mu^{\#}=(I_p-A^{\#}/\beta^{\#})\lambda^{\mathrm{WH}}
 =(I_p-A/\beta)\lambda=\mu.
\tag{24}
\]
This proves exact recovery of the structural triple and of \(G(A)=\{j\to i:A_{ij}>0\}\); the latter equality is the graph definition, not an identification assumption.

For basis invariance, let \(O'=OU\), where \(U\) is orthogonal.  Direct substitution and uniqueness of the principal logarithm yield
\[
 R'=U^{\mathsf T}R,\quad F'=U^{\mathsf T}FU,\quad
 L^{\mathrm{WH}\prime}=U^{\mathsf T}L^{\mathrm{WH}}U,\quad
 H^{\mathrm{WH}\prime}=U^{\mathsf T}H^{\mathrm{WH}}U,\quad
 P^{\mathrm{WH}\prime}=U^{\mathsf T}P^{\mathrm{WH}}.
\tag{25}
\]
Consequently \(K^{\mathrm{WH}}\), \(C_{+}^{\mathrm{WH}}\), \(\mathcal R^{\mathrm{WH}}\), and \(g^{\mathrm{WH}}\) are unchanged.  The first equation in (17) after the basis change is obtained by right-multiplying its original version by \(U\), so the candidate set and its unique pair are unchanged.  Every operation and the final output are therefore basis invariant.  Since the admissible representative at the start was arbitrary, equations (1)--(24) force every representative of the same observed moment triple to equal this uniquely recovered triple; this proves representative invariance.

Finally, consider \(p=2\), arbitrary \(\Delta>0\), \(\beta=1\), \(\mu=\mathbf 1_2\), and \(A=\tfrac14\mathbf 1_2\mathbf 1_2^{\mathsf T}\).  Its eigenvalues are \(1/2\) and \(0\), so \(\rho(A/\beta)=1/2<1\).  A direct stationary immigration--birth construction is available: immigrants of each type form homogeneous Poisson processes of rate one, and every type-\(j\) point produces independent type-\(i\) Poisson offspring at delay density \(A_{ij}e^{-t}\mathbf 1_{\{t>0\}}\).  The expected total descendants are bounded by the entrywise-finite series \(\sum_{d\geq0}A^d=(I_2-A)^{-1}\); hence every cluster is finite almost surely, and Campbell's formula gives finite expected counts on bounded intervals for their stationary superposition, hence local finiteness almost surely.  Translation invariance gives stationarity, continuous offspring delays give simplicity, and summing the independent offspring hazards gives the stated conditional intensity.  Thus the construction is a member of \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,2}^{\mathrm{stab}}\).  Its image is \(\operatorname{span}(\mathbf 1_2)\), and \(B\) restricts there to multiplication by \(-1/2\), so the restricted spectrum is real and satisfies the strip condition for every \(\Delta>0\).  The matrix \(A\) has determinant zero, positive diagonal entries, positive reciprocal off-diagonal entries, and non-coordinate image, which proves the final strictness example.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} It gives a finite matrix-resolvent and unique-positive-root population inverse on the broad stable restricted-strip class, including singular, reciprocal, cyclic, and self-exciting mechanisms. \textit{Gap.} BacryMuzy2016 identifies the kernel from the complete conditional response; this theorem reconstructs that response from only the mean and two fixed-bin covariance lags and turns Wiener--Hopf uniqueness into a finite root certificate. \textit{Consumer.} Coarsely timestamped multichannel alarm systems can be structurally identified beyond feed-forward graphs at population level when the restricted principal-log strip is scientifically credible.

\begin{theorem}[thm:stable-nonzero-covariance-only-wiener-hopf-recovery]\label{thm:stable-nonzero-covariance-only-wiener-hopf-recovery}
For every \(\gamma=(\Gamma_0,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\in\mathfrak J_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{WH}}\), every operation in \(\text{def:wiener-hopf-covariance-only-inverse}\) is well defined, independent of the orthonormal basis and of the admissible structural representative, and reconstructs
\[
 r=\operatorname{rank}(A),\quad S=\operatorname{im}(A),\quad
 F=[T|_S]_O,\quad L^{\mathrm{WH}}=[B|_S]_O,\quad
 H^{\mathrm{WH}}=[J|_S]_O^{-1},\quad K^{\mathrm{WH}}=K,
\]
\[
 C_{+}^{\mathrm{WH}}(t)=C_{+}(t)\quad(t>0),\quad
 D^{\mathrm{WH},0}=D,\quad
 m^{\mathrm{WH},0}=m.
\]
The revised mean-plus-two-lag evaluator then uses only its finite-dimensional response transform and its unique positive scalar root.  For every admissible representative,
\[
 \Psi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{WH},0}(\Gamma_0,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)
 =(\beta,A,\mu).
\]
Thus \(\Gamma_0,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2\) alone identify the same stable nonzero restricted-strip regime.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(\gamma=(\Gamma_0,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\in\mathfrak J_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{WH}}\), and choose an arbitrary admissible representative \((\beta,A,\mu)\) with population mean \(m\).  By \(\text{def:stable-nonzero-restricted-strip-covariance-image}\), the representative is stable, satisfies \(A\neq0\) and the restricted principal-log strip, and its covariances equal the supplied triple.  Therefore
\[
 \eta=(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\in\mathfrak I_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{WH}}
\tag{1}
\]
by \(\text{def:stable-nonzero-restricted-strip-moment-image}\).

Apply \(\text{thm:stable-nonzero-two-lag-wiener-hopf-recovery}\) to (1).  The part of its proof through reconstruction of \(C_{+}^{\mathrm{WH}}\) uses only \((\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)\), and establishes
\[
 r=\operatorname{rank}(A),\quad S=\operatorname{im}(A),\quad
 F=[T|_S]_O,\quad L^{\mathrm{WH}}=[B|_S]_O,
\tag{2}
\]
\[
 H^{\mathrm{WH}}=[J|_S]_O^{-1},\quad K^{\mathrm{WH}}=K,\quad
 C_{+}^{\mathrm{WH}}(t)=C_{+}(t)\quad(t>0).
\tag{3}
\]
That theorem also proves basis invariance of all quantities in (2)--(3).

The curve in (3) extends continuously to zero, so the finite-interval integral in \(\text{def:wiener-hopf-covariance-only-inverse}\) exists.  Substituting (3) into \(\text{lem:zero-lag-bin-covariance}\) gives
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 D^{\mathrm{WH},0}
 &=\Delta^{-1}\!\left[\Gamma_0-
 \int_0^\Delta(\Delta-t)
 \{C_{+}^{\mathrm{WH}}(t)+C_{+}^{\mathrm{WH}}(t)^{\mathsf T}\}\,dt\right]\\
 &=\Delta^{-1}\!\left[\Gamma_0-
 \int_0^\Delta(\Delta-t)
 \{C_{+}(t)+C_{+}(t)^{\mathsf T}\}\,dt\right]=D\succ0.
 \end{aligned}
\tag{4}
\]
Hence \(\lambda^{\mathrm{WH},0}=\operatorname{diag}(D)=\lambda\), and the mean identity in the same lemma yields
\[
 m^{\mathrm{WH},0}=\Delta\lambda=m.
\tag{5}
\]
The argument passed to \(\Phi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{WH}}\) by the covariance-only definition is therefore exactly the element (1).  The finite-dimensional transform/root theorem applies and gives
\[
 \Psi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{WH},0}(\gamma)
 =\Phi_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{WH}}(m,\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2)
 =(\beta,A,\mu).
\tag{6}
\]
Equations (2)--(6) prove totality of every covariance-only operation.  Their left sides depend only on \(\gamma\), the known positive bin width \(\Delta\), and an inessential basis.  Since the admissible representative was arbitrary, (6) also forces every representative to equal the same recovered triple, proving representative independence and exact identification from the three covariance matrices alone.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} It reconstructs the innovation intensity and mean from zero lag, then invokes the same finite matrix-resolvent and positive-root evaluator. \textit{Gap.} Complete-covariance identification does not isolate this exact three-covariance fixed-bin inverse on the broad singular stable regime. \textit{Consumer.} Archived alarm covariance summaries remain population-identifying even when marginal count means were not retained.

\section{Interpretation}

For fully observed labeled alarm channels, an entry \(A_{ij}>0\) is the excitation of receiver \(i\) by sender \(j\) in the stipulated continuous-time Hawkes mechanism. The theorem identifies that mechanism from coarse summaries; it does not by itself rule out hidden common causes or validate a Hawkes model for the physical alarm system. For Qiao et al.'s metropolitan cellular-alarm logs at 1--9 second resolution, the result explains when coarse timestamps preserve the continuous-time directed mechanism exactly at population level and when numerical conditioning or model misspecification still requires caution.

\section*{Technical internal limitation (diagnostic only)}

The broad population inverse reduces to a finite-dimensional matrix-resolvent calculation and a unique positive scalar root. This note proves exact population totality but no uniform conditioning, sample estimator, rate, or confidence theorem for that root on the broad feedback class. Existing inference remains acyclic-only.

\section{Honest scope}

The principal exact and inference results concern stationary labeled linear Hawkes laws with known fixed \(\Delta\), positive baselines, nonnegative excitation, one common exponential decay, the contribution-defining feed-forward acyclicity restriction, and at least one edge. Under the loop-inclusive graph convention, acyclicity implies \(A_{ii}=0\) and nilpotence implies stability. A separate population-only extension drops acyclicity, zero diagonal, coordinate support, and full rank: it identifies every nonzero law in \(\mathcal M_{\Delta,p}^{\mathrm{stab}}\) for which \(\Delta(A-\beta I_p)|_{\operatorname{im}(A)}\) lies in the principal-log strip. That extension is evaluated by finite matrix operations and a unique positive scalar root, but this note gives no uniform conditioning, estimator, rate, or confidence theorem for the broad root. At \(A=0\), the process reduces to independent Poisson counts and \(\beta\) is unidentified. The bounded-Lipschitz Gaussian approximation, HAC consistency, vanishing-threshold support recovery, and fixed-level simultaneous graph-inclusion coverage are acyclic-only; the fixed-level set has a conservative \(\liminf\) guarantee, not exact coverage or support recovery. Boundary honesty remains open over the nuisance-regular acyclic closure, and one-lag injectivity remains open on the named three-node chain, fork, and diamond supports. Heterogeneous decays, hidden labels, growing \(p\), and sample inference for feedback mechanisms remain unresolved.
